   107	          if [[ $file -nt $HMACfile ]]; then
   108	            ${rpcauth} "$user" "$file" | grep rpcauth | cut -d ':' -f 2 > "$HMACfile"
   109	          fi
   110	        }
   111	        makeCert() {
   112	          name=$1
   113	          # Add leading comma if not empty
   114	          extraAltNames=''${2:+,}''${2:-}
   115	          if [[ ! -e $name-key ]]; then
   116	            # Create new key and cert
   117	            doMakeCert "-newkey ec -pkeyopt ec_paramgen_curve:prime256v1 -nodes -keyout $name-key"
   118	          elif [[ ! -e $name-cert \
   119	                  || $(cat "$name-cert-alt-names" 2>/dev/null) != $extraAltNames ]]; then
   120	            # Create cert from existing key
   121	            doMakeCert "-key $name-key"
   122	          fi;
   123	        }
   124	        doMakeCert() {
   125	           # This fn uses global variables `name` and `extraAltNames`
   126	           keyOpts=$1
   127	           ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl req -x509 \
   128	             -sha256 -days 3650 $keyOpts -out "$name-cert"  \
   129	             -subj "/CN=localhost/O=$name" \
   130	             -addext "subjectAltName=DNS:localhost,IP:127.0.0.1$extraAltNames"
   131	           echo "$extraAltNames" > "$name-cert-alt-names"
   132	        }
   133	
   134	        umask u=rw,go=
   135	        ${builtins.concatStringsSep "\n" (builtins.attrValues cfg.generateSecretsCmds)}
   136	      '';
   137	    };
   138	  };
   139	
   140	  cfg = config.nix-bitcoin;
   141	in {
   142	  inherit options;
   143	
   144	  config = {
   145	    assertions = [
   146	      { assertion = cfg.secretsSetupMethod != null;
   147	        message = ''
   148	          No secrets setup method has been defined.
   149	          To fix this, choose one of the following:
   150	
   151	           - Use one of the deployment methods in ${toString ./../deployment}
   152	
   153	           - Set `nix-bitcoin.generateSecrets = true` to automatically generate secrets
   154	
   155	           - Set `nix-bitcoin.secretsSetupMethod = "manual"` if you want to manually setup secrets
   156	        '';
   157	      }
   158	    ];
   159	
   160	    # This target is active when secrets have been setup successfully.
   161	    systemd.targets.nix-bitcoin-secrets = mkIf (cfg.secretsSetupMethod != "manual") {
   162	      # This ensures that the secrets target is always activated when switching
   163	      # configurations.
   164	      # In this way `switch-to-configuration` is guaranteed to show an error
   165	      # when activating the secrets target fails on deployment.
   166	      wantedBy = [ "multi-user.target" ];
   167	    };
   168	
   169	    nix-bitcoin.setupSecrets = mkIf cfg.generateSecrets true;
   170	
   171	    nix-bitcoin.secretsSetupMethod = mkIf cfg.setupSecrets "setup-secrets";
   172	
   173	    # Operation of this service:
   174	    #  - Set owner and permissions for all used secrets
   175	    #  - Make all other secrets accessible to root only
   176	    # For all steps make sure that no secrets are copied to the nix store.
   177	    #
   178	    # The secrets dir must exist before the sandboxed service starts:
   179	    # ReadWritePaths= refuses a missing path.
   180	    systemd.tmpfiles.rules = mkIf cfg.setupSecrets [
   181	      "d '${cfg.secretsDir}' 0700 root root - -"
   182	    ];
   183	
   184	    systemd.services.setup-secrets = mkIf cfg.setupSecrets {
   185	      requiredBy = [ "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target" ];
   186	      before = [ "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target" ];
   187	      serviceConfig = {
   188	         Type = "oneshot";
   189	         RemainAfterExit = true;
   190	         # Scoped root sandbox (audit 2026-09-09, low): this is the one root
   191	         # service that generates and re-permissions every secret on the box.
   192	         # It needs to write exactly one directory and to chown/chmod files it
   193	         # does not own — nothing else. No network, no new privileges, no
   194	         # writable / outside the secrets dir.
   195	         ProtectSystem = "strict";
   196	         ReadWritePaths = [ cfg.secretsDir ];
   197	         ProtectHome = true;
   198	         PrivateTmp = true;
   199	         NoNewPrivileges = true;
   200	         RestrictAddressFamilies = [ "AF_UNIX" ];
   201	         RestrictNamespaces = true;
   202	         LockPersonality = true;
   203	         ProtectKernelTunables = true;
   204	         ProtectKernelModules = true;
   205	         ProtectControlGroups = true;
   206	         CapabilityBoundingSet = [ "CAP_CHOWN" "CAP_FOWNER" "CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE" "CAP_DAC_READ_SEARCH" ];
   207	      };
   208	      script = ''
   209	        # Use the same sort order for globbing and sorting as in Nix attrsets.
   210	        # Required for `comm` below.
   211	        export LC_COLLATE=C
   212	
   213	        ${optionalString cfg.generateSecrets ''
   214	          mkdir -p "${cfg.secretsDir}"
   215	          cd "${cfg.secretsDir}"
   216	          chown root: .
   217	          chmod 0700 .
   218	          ${cfg.generateSecretsScript}
   219	        ''}
   220	
   221	        setupSecret() {
   222	           file="$1"
   223	           user="$2"
   224	           group="$3"
   225	           permissions="$4"
   226	           if [[ ! -e $file ]]; then
   227	             echo "Error: Secret file '$file' is missing"
   228	             exit 1
   229	           fi
   230	           chown "$user:$group" "$file"
   231	           chmod "$permissions" "$file"
   232	           processedFiles+=("$file")
   233	        }
   234	
   235	        dir="${cfg.secretsDir}"
   236	        if [[ ! -e $dir ]]; then
   237	          echo "Error: Secrets dir '$dir' is missing"
   238	          exit 1
   239	        fi
   240	        chown root: "$dir"
   241	        cd "$dir"
   242	
   243	        processedFiles=()
   244	        ${
   245	          concatStrings (mapAttrsToList (n: v: ''
   246	            setupSecret ${escapeShellArg n} ${escapeShellArg v.user} ${escapeShellArg v.group} ${escapeShellArg v.permissions}
   247	          '') cfg.secrets)
   248	        }
   249	
   250	        # Make all other files accessible to root only.
   251	        # dotglob: a stray `.something` in the secrets dir must be locked down
   252	        # too, not skipped by the glob (audit 2026-08-30, low).
   253	        unprocessedFiles=$(
   254	          comm -23 <(shopt -s nullglob dotglob; printf '%s\n' * | sort) <(printf '%s\n' "''${processedFiles[@]}" | sort)
   255	        )
   256	        if [[ $unprocessedFiles ]]; then
   257	          IFS=$'\n'
   258	          # shellcheck disable=SC2086
   259	          chown root: $unprocessedFiles
   260	          # shellcheck disable=SC2086
   261	          chmod 0440 $unprocessedFiles
   262	        fi
   263	
   264	        # Now make the secrets dir accessible to other users
   265	        chmod 0751 "$dir"
   266	      '';
   267	    };
   268	  };
   269	}

     2	# By default, onion addresses in /var/lib/tor/onion are only readable by the

    17	        For example, the following allows user 'myuser' to access bitcoind

    19	        ```nix
    20	        {
    21	          "myuser" = [ "bitcoind" "lnd" ];
    22	        };
    23	        ```
    24	        The onion hostnames can then be read from

    27	    };

    56	        StateDirectory = "onion-addresses";
    57	        StateDirectoryMode = "771";
    58	        PrivateNetwork = true; # This service needs no network access
    59	        PrivateUsers = false;
    60	        CapabilityBoundingSet = "CAP_CHOWN CAP_FSETID CAP_SETFCAP CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE CAP_DAC_READ_SEARCH CAP_FOWNER CAP_IPC_OWNER";
    61	      };
    62	      # This service runs as root with CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE/CAP_DAC_READ_SEARCH/
    63	      # CAP_CHOWN, so it must never follow an attacker-planted symlink: doing so
    64	      # would let a compromised service user redirect a read or a chown to an
    65	      # arbitrary path (e.g. copy a macaroon out, or take ownership of /etc/shadow).
    66	      # Two rules enforce this:
    67	      #   1. Refuse to read a Tor hostname file that is a symlink.
    68	      #   2. Build each output directory as root:root, fully populate it, and
    69	      #      only THEN hand ownership to the target user — so the user never has
    70	      #      write access to the directory while root is still writing into it,
    71	      #      and there is no window to race a symlink in. All chowns use `-h` so
    72	      #      a symlink is never dereferenced even if one somehow appears.
    73	      script = ''
    74	        set -euo pipefail
    75	
    76	        waitForFile() {
    77	          local file=$1
    78	          for ((i=0; i<300; i++)); do
    79	            if [[ -e $file ]]; then
    80	              return
    81	            fi
    82	            sleep 0.1
    83	          done
    84	          echo "Error: File $file did not appear after 30 sec." >&2
    85	          exit 1
    86	        }
    87	
    88	        # Copy a Tor hostname file, refusing to read it through a symlink and
    89	        # refusing to copy anything that is not exactly one v3 onion address.
    90	        #
    91	        # Content validation is load-bearing, not cosmetic: this file's bytes are
    92	        # spliced verbatim into bitcoin.conf/lnd.conf (`externalip=$(cat …)`) and
    93	        # into the lndconnect admin-macaroon URL (`--host=$(cat …)`). A tor-user
    94	        # who can write /var/lib/tor/onion/<svc>/hostname could otherwise plant a
    95	        # second line (`shutdownnotify=…`, `bitcoind.rpchost=…`) and get it
    96	        # executed / honoured downstream. Requiring a single `<56×base32>.onion`
    97	        # line fails closed on any such payload.
    98	        #
    99	        # It also closes the check-then-read TOCTOU: we read the file ONCE into a
   100	        # variable and validate that copy, so even if a symlink is raced in after
   101	        # the -L check, a secret's contents can never match the onion shape and
   102	        # are never written to the service-owned output.
   103	        copyOnionFile() {
   104	          local src=$1 dst=$2 content
   105	          waitForFile "$src"
   106	          if [[ -L $src ]]; then
   107	            echo "Error: refusing to read onion hostname via symlink: $src" >&2
   108	            exit 1
   109	          fi
   110	          content=$(cat "$src")
   111	          if [[ ! $content =~ ^[a-z2-7]{56}[.]onion$ ]]; then
   112	            echo "Error: $src does not contain exactly one v3 onion address; refusing to copy" >&2
   113	            exit 1
   114	          fi
   115	          install -T -m 400 <(printf '%s\n' "$content") "$dst"
   116	        }
   117	
   118	        # Wait until tor is up
   119	        waitForFile /var/lib/tor/state
   120	
   121	        if [[ -L ${cfg.dataDir} ]]; then
   122	          echo "Error: ${cfg.dataDir} is a symlink" >&2
   123	          exit 1
   124	        fi
   125	        cd ${cfg.dataDir}
   126	        rm -rf -- ./*
   127	
   128	        ${concatMapStrings
   129	          (user: ''
   130	            # Create the dir as root:root 0700 and keep it that way while writing:
   131	            # the target user cannot enter it, so cannot plant a symlink to race.
   132	            mkdir -p -m 0700 -- '${user}'
   133	            ${concatMapStrings
   134	              (service: ''
   135	                copyOnionFile '/var/lib/tor/onion/${service}/hostname' '${user}/${service}'
   136	                chown -h '${user}' '${user}/${service}'
   137	              '')
   138	              cfg.access.${user}
   139	             }
   140	            # Hand the fully-populated directory to the user last.
   141	            chown -h '${user}' '${user}'
   142	          '')
   143	          (builtins.attrNames cfg.access)
   144	        }
   145	
   146	        ${optionalString (cfg.services != []) ''
   147	          # Root-owned, world-traversable (not writable): each service reads only
   148	          # its own 0400 file by exact path; no user can plant symlinks here.
   149	          mkdir -p -m 0755 -- services
   150	          ${concatMapStrings (service: ''
   151	            copyOnionFile '/var/lib/tor/onion/${service}/hostname' 'services/${service}'
   152	            chown -h '${config.systemd.services.${service}.serviceConfig.User}' 'services/${service}'
   153	          '') cfg.services}
   154	        ''}
   155	      '';
   156	    };
   157	  };
   158	}
     1	# This module creates onion-services for NixOS services.
     2	# An onion service can be enabled for every service that defines
     3	# options 'address', 'port' and optionally 'getPublicAddressCmd'.
     4	#
     5	# See it in use at ./presets/enable-tor.nix
     6	
     7	{ config, lib, pkgs, ... }:
     8	
     9	with lib;
    10	let
    11	  options.nix-bitcoin.onionServices = mkOption {
    12	    default = {};
    13	    type = with types; attrsOf (submodule (
    14	      { config, ... }: {
    15	        options = {
    16	          enable = mkOption {
    17	            type = types.bool;
    18	            default = config.public;
    19	            description = ''
    20	              Create an onion service for the given service.
    21	              The service must define options {option}`address` and {option}`onionPort` (or `port`).
    22	            '';
    23	          };
    24	          public = mkOption {
    25	            type = types.bool;
    26	            default = false;
    27	            description = ''
    28	              Make the onion address accessible to the service.
    29	              If enabled, the onion service is automatically enabled.
    30	              Only available for services that define option {option}`getPublicAddressCmd`.
    31	            '';
    32	          };
    33	          externalPort = mkOption {
    34	            type = types.nullOr types.port;
    35	            default = null;
    36	            description = "Override the external port of the onion service.";
    37	          };
    38	        };
    39	      }
    40	    ));
    41	  };
    42	
    43	  cfg = config.nix-bitcoin.onionServices;
    44	  nbLib = config.nix-bitcoin.lib;
    45	
    46	  onionServices = builtins.attrNames cfg;
    47	
    48	  activeServices = builtins.filter (service:
    49	    config.services.${service}.enable && cfg.${service}.enable
    50	  ) onionServices;
    51	
    52	  publicServices = builtins.filter (service: cfg.${service}.public) activeServices;
    53	in {
    54	  inherit options;
    55	
    56	  config = mkMerge [
    57	    (mkIf (activeServices != []) {
    58	      # Define hidden services
    59	      services.tor = {
    60	        enable = true;
    61	        relay.onionServices = genAttrs activeServices (name:
    62	          let
    63	            service = config.services.${name};
    64	            inherit (cfg.${name}) externalPort;
    65	          in nbLib.mkOnionService {
    66	            port = if externalPort != null then externalPort else service.port;
    67	            target.port = service.onionPort or service.port;
    68	            target.addr = nbLib.address service.address;
    69	          }
    70	        );
    71	      };
    72	
    73	      nix-bitcoin.onionAddresses = {
    74	        # Enable public services to access their own onion addresses
    75	        services = publicServices;
    76	
    77	        # Allow the operator user to access onion addresses for all active services
    78	        access.${config.nix-bitcoin.operator.name} = mkIf config.nix-bitcoin.operator.enable activeServices;
    79	      };
    80	      systemd.services = let
    81	        onionAddresses = [ "onion-addresses.service" ];
    82	      in genAttrs publicServices (service: {
    83	        # TODO-EXTERNAL: Instead of `wants`, use a future systemd dependency type
    84	        # that propagates initial start failures but no restarts
    85	        wants = onionAddresses;
    86	        after = onionAddresses;
    87	      });
    88	    })
    89	
    90	    # Set getPublicAddressCmd for public services
    91	    {
    92	      services = let
    93	        # publicServices' doesn't depend on config.services.*.enable,
    94	        # so we can use it to define config.services without causing infinite recursion
    95	        publicServices' = builtins.filter (service:
    96	          let srv = cfg.${service};
    97	          in srv.public && srv.enable
    98	        ) onionServices;
    99	      in genAttrs publicServices' (service: {
   100	        getPublicAddressCmd = "cat ${config.nix-bitcoin.onionAddresses.dataDir}/services/${service}";
   101	      });
   102	    }
   103	
   104	    # Set sensible defaults for some services
   105	    {
   106	      nix-bitcoin.onionServices = {
   107	        btcpayserver = {
   108	          externalPort = 80;
   109	        };
   110	      };
   111	    }
   112	  ];
   113	}
     1	{ config, lib, pkgs, ... }:
     2	
     3	with lib;
     4	{
     5	  options = {
     6	    nix-bitcoin.security.dbusHideProcessInformation = mkOption {
     7	      type = types.bool;
     8	      default = false;
     9	      description = ''
    10	        Only allow users with group `proc` to retrieve systemd unit information like
    11	        cgroup paths (i.e. (sub)process command lines) via D-Bus.
    12	
    13	        This mitigates a systemd security issue where (sub)process command lines can
    14	        be retrieved by services even when their access to /proc is restricted
    15	        (via ProtectProc).
    16	
    17	        This option works by restricting the D-Bus method `GetUnitProcesses`, which
    18	        is also used internally by {command}`systemctl status`.
    19	      '';
    20	    };
    21	  };
    22	
    23	  config = mkIf config.nix-bitcoin.security.dbusHideProcessInformation {
    24	    users.groups.proc = {};
    25	    nix-bitcoin.operator.groups = [ "proc" ]; # Enable operator access to systemd-status
    26	
    27	    services.dbus.packages = lib.mkAfter [ # Apply at the end to override the default policy
    28	      (pkgs.writeTextDir "etc/dbus-1/system.d/dbus.conf" ''
    29	        <busconfig>
    30	          <policy context="default">
    31	            <deny
    32	              send_destination="org.freedesktop.systemd1"
    33	              send_interface="org.freedesktop.systemd1.Manager"
    34	              send_member="GetUnitProcesses"
    35	            />
    36	          </policy>
    37	          <policy group="proc">
    38	            <allow
    39	              send_destination="org.freedesktop.systemd1"
    40	              send_interface="org.freedesktop.systemd1.Manager"
    41	              send_member="GetUnitProcesses"
    42	            />
    43	          </policy>
    44	        </busconfig>
    45	      '')
    46	    ];
    47	  };
    48	}

     3	# Create a WireGuard server with a single peer.
     4	# Private/public keys are created via the secrets system.
     5	# Add helper binaries `nix-bitcoin-wg-connect` and optionally `lndconnect-wg`.
     6	
     7	# See ../../docs/services.md ("Use Zeus (mobile lightning wallet) via WireGuard")
     8	# for usage instructions.
     9	
    10	# This is a rather opinionated implementation that lacks the flexibility offered by
    11	# other nix-bitcoin modules, so ship this as a `preset`.
    12	# Some users will prefer to use `lndconnect` with their existing WireGuard or Tailscale setup.
    13	
    14	with lib;
    15	let
    16	  options.nix-bitcoin.wireguard = {
    17	    subnet = mkOption {
    18	      type = types.str;
    19	      default = "10.10.0";
    20	      description = "The /24 subnet of the wireguard network.";
    21	    };
    22	    restrictPeer = mkOption {
    23	      type = types.bool;
    24	      default = true;
    25	      description = ''
    26	        Prevent the peer from connecting to any addresses except for the WireGuard server address.
    27	      '';
    28	    };
    29	  };
    30	
    31	  cfg = config.nix-bitcoin.wireguard;
    32	  wgSubnet = cfg.subnet;
    33	  inherit (config.networking.wireguard.interfaces) wg-nb;
    34	  inherit (config.services) lnd;
    35	
    36	  lndconnect = lnd.enable && lnd.lndconnect.enable;
    37	
    38	  serverAddress = "${wgSubnet}.1";
    39	  peerAddress = "${wgSubnet}.2";
    40	
    41	  secretsDir = config.nix-bitcoin.secretsDir;
    42	
    43	  wgConnectUser = if config.nix-bitcoin.operator.enable
    44	                  then config.nix-bitcoin.operator.name
    45	                  else "root";
    46	
    47	  # A script that prints a QR code to connect a peer to the server.
    48	  # The QR code encodes a wg-quick config that can be imported by the wireguard
    49	  # mobile app.
    50	  wgConnect = pkgs.writers.writeBashBin "nix-bitcoin-wg-connect" ''
    51	    set -euo pipefail
    52	    text=
    53	    host=
    54	    for arg in "$@"; do
    55	      case $arg in
    56	        --text)
    57	          text=1
    58	          ;;
    59	        *)
    60	          host=$arg
    61	          ;;
    62	      esac
    63	    done
    64	
    65	    if [[ ! $host ]]; then
    66	      # Use lndconnect to fetch the external ip.
    67	      # This internally uses https://github.com/GlenDC/go-external-ip, which
    68	      # queries a set of external ip providers.
    69	      host=$(
    70	        ${getExe config.nix-bitcoin.pkgs.lndconnect} --url --nocert \
    71	          --configfile=/dev/null --adminmacaroonpath=/dev/null \
    72	          | sed -nE 's|.*?/(.*?):.*|\1|p'
    73	      )
    74	    fi
    75	
    76	    config="[Interface]
    77	    PrivateKey = $(cat ${secretsDir}/wg-peer-private-key)
    78	    Address = ${peerAddress}/24
    79	
    80	    [Peer]
    81	    PublicKey = $(cat ${secretsDir}/wg-server-public-key)
    82	    AllowedIPs = ${wgSubnet}.0/24
    83	    Endpoint = $host:${toString wg-nb.listenPort}
    84	    PersistentKeepalive = 25
    85	    "
    86	
    87	    if [[ $text ]]; then
    88	      echo "$config"
    89	    else
    90	      echo "$config" | ${getExe pkgs.qrencode} -t UTF8 -o -
    91	    fi
    92	  '';
    93	in {
    94	  inherit options;
    95	
    96	  config = {
    97	    assertions = [
    98	      {
    99	        # Don't support `netns-isolation` for now to keep things simple
   100	        assertion = !(config.nix-bitcoin.netns-isolation.enable or false);
   101	        message = "`nix-bitcoin.wireguard` is not compatible with `netns-isolation`.";
   102	      }
   103	      # The whole security model of this preset is the iptables rule set
   104	      # below: the lnd admin REST listener is bound to 0.0.0.0 (see the comment
   105	      # at `restAddress`) and the peer restriction is an iptables REJECT. Two
   106	      # silent ways to lose that (audit 2026-09-09): the firewall disabled, or
   107	      # the nftables backend enabled — NixOS then ignores `extraCommands`
   108	      # without an error, so the REST accept rule and the restrictPeer REJECT
   109	      # simply vanish. Refuse to evaluate rather than ship an unguarded admin API.
   110	      {
   111	        # restrictPeer is also only an iptables REJECT: with the firewall off it
   112	        # evaluates fine and installs nothing (audit second opinion 2026-10-01).
   113	        assertion = !(lndconnect || cfg.restrictPeer) || config.networking.firewall.enable;
   114	        message = "nix-bitcoin wireguard preset: services.lnd.lndconnect binds the admin REST API to 0.0.0.0 and `restrictPeer` is an iptables REJECT; both rely on networking.firewall — enable the firewall.";
   115	      }
   116	      {
   117	        # Check the effective backend, not the enable flags that feed its
   118	        # default: `networking.firewall.backend` can also be set directly, and
   119	        # both the nftables and the firewalld backends drop `extraCommands`
   120	        # without an error (audits 2026-10-01 and 2026-10-04).
   121	        assertion = (config.networking.firewall.backend or "iptables") == "iptables";
   122	        message = "nix-bitcoin wireguard preset: its rules are iptables `extraCommands`, which the nftables and firewalld backends silently drop (networking.firewall.backend = \"${config.networking.firewall.backend or "iptables"}\"). Keep the iptables backend or port the rules.";
   123	      }
   124	    ];
   125	
   126	    networking.wireguard.interfaces.wg-nb = {
   127	      ips = [ "${serverAddress}/24" ];
   128	      listenPort = mkDefault 51820;
   129	      privateKeyFile = "${secretsDir}/wg-server-private-key";
   130	      allowedIPsAsRoutes = false;
   131	      peers = [
   132	        {
   133	          # To use the actual public key from the secrets file, use dummy pubkey
   134	          # `peer0` and replace it via `getPubkeyFromFile` (see further below)
   135	          # at peer service runtime.
   136	          publicKey = "peer0";
   137	          allowedIPs = [ "${peerAddress}/32" ];
   138	        }
   139	      ];
   140	    };
   141	
   142	    systemd.services = {
   143	      wireguard-wg-nb = rec {
   144	        wants = [ "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target" ];
   145	        after = wants;
   146	      };
   147	
   148	      # Read the peer pubkey from the secrets file at service runtime.
   149	      # (Upstream patched its own source with sed and re-executed it as root,
   150	      # which turned a secrets-store write into root RCE; audit L-8.)
   151	      wireguard-wg-nb-peer-peer0 = {
   152	        script = mkForce ''
   153	          ${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg set wg-nb peer "$(cat "${secretsDir}/wg-peer-public-key")" allowed-ips ${peerAddress}/32
   154	        '';
   155	        postStop = mkForce ''
   156	          ${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg set wg-nb peer "$(cat "${secretsDir}/wg-peer-public-key")" remove || true
   157	        '';
   158	      };
   159	    };
   160	
   161	    environment.systemPackages = [
   162	      wgConnect
   163	    ] ++ (optional lndconnect
   164	      (pkgs.writers.writeBashBin "lndconnect-wg" ''
   165	        exec lndconnect --host "${serverAddress}" --nocert "$@"
   166	      '')
   167	    );
   168	
   169	    networking.firewall = let
   170	      restrictPeerRule = "-s ${peerAddress} ! -d ${serverAddress} -j REJECT";
   171	    in {
   172	      allowedUDPPorts = [ wg-nb.listenPort ];
   173	
   174	      extraCommands =
   175	        optionalString lndconnect ''
   176	          # Admit lnd REST only from the single WireGuard peer, and only when the
   177	          # packet actually arrived over the wg-nb interface. `-s` alone matches
   178	          # a spoofed source on any interface; `-i` pins it to the tunnel (audit
   179	          # 2026-08-29, low). This is the interim hardening while the
   180	          # 0.0.0.0 bind itself stays deferred (see docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md).
   181	          iptables -w -A nixos-fw -i wg-nb -p tcp -s ${peerAddress}/32 --dport ${toString lnd.restPort} -j nixos-fw-accept
   182	        ''
   183	        + optionalString cfg.restrictPeer ''
   184	          # Insert at the top: appended (-A) the REJECT would sit behind the
   185	          # standard accept rules (allowed ports, the REST accept above), so a
   186	          # peer could still reach any firewall-allowed port on the node's
   187	          # other addresses through the tunnel (second opinion 2026-09-09).
   188	          # Replies to server-initiated traffic are unaffected: their
   189	          # destination is serverAddress.
   190	          iptables -w -I nixos-fw 1 ${restrictPeerRule}
   191	          iptables -w -I FORWARD 1 ${restrictPeerRule}
   192	        '';
   193	
   194	      extraStopCommands =
   195	        # Rules added to chain `nixos-fw` are automatically removed when restarting
   196	        # the NixOS firewall service.
   197	        mkIf cfg.restrictPeer ''
   198	          iptables -w -D FORWARD ${restrictPeerRule} || :
   199	        '';
   200	    };
   201	
   202	    # Listen on all addresses, including `serverAddress`.
   203	    # The listen port is guarded by the firewall (nixos-fw default-drops; only
   204	    # the wg subnet accept rule above admits REST traffic), but this is still
   205	    # a known audit finding (2026-08-24, advisory): the admin REST API relies
   206	    # on the firewall alone. Binding to `serverAddress` instead is DEFERRED —
   207	    # `nbLib.address` maps 0.0.0.0 to 127.0.0.1, so every internal consumer
   208	    # of `restAddress` (macaroon ExecStartPost, btcpayserver, lndconnect
   209	    # onion target) silently depends on this value, and a naive rebind breaks
   210	    # them plus lnd startup ordering. Read docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md and
   211	    # work through its checklist before changing this line.
   212	    services.lnd = mkIf lndconnect {
   213	      restAddress = "0.0.0.0";
   214	      tor.enforce = false;
   215	    };
   216	
   217	    nix-bitcoin.secrets = {
   218	      wg-server-private-key = {};
   219	      wg-server-public-key = { user = wgConnectUser; group = "root"; };
   220	      wg-peer-private-key = { user = wgConnectUser; group = "root"; };
   221	      wg-peer-public-key = {};
   222	    };
   223	
   224	    nix-bitcoin.generateSecretsCmds.wireguard = let
   225	      wg = "${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg";
   226	    in ''
   227	      makeWireguardKey() {
   228	        local name=$1
   229	        local priv=wg-$name-private-key
   230	        local pub=wg-$name-public-key
   231	        if [[ ! -e $priv ]]; then
   232	          ${wg} genkey > $priv
   233	        fi
   234	        if [[ $priv -nt $pub ]]; then
   235	          ${wg} pubkey < $priv > $pub
   236	        fi
   237	      }
   238	      makeWireguardKey server
   239	      makeWireguardKey peer
   240	    '';
   241	  };

   337	      '') (builtins.attrValues cfg.rpc.users)

   339	    ${lib.concatMapStrings (rpcallowip: "rpcallowip=${rpcallowip}\n") cfg.rpc.allowip}
   340	
   341	    # Wallet options
   342	    ${optionalString (cfg.addresstype != null) "addresstype=${cfg.addresstype}"}
   343	
   344	    # ZMQ options
   345	    ${optionalString (cfg.zmqpubrawblock != null) "zmqpubrawblock=${cfg.zmqpubrawblock}"}
   346	    ${optionalString (cfg.zmqpubrawtx != null) "zmqpubrawtx=${cfg.zmqpubrawtx}"}
   347	
   348	    # Extra options
   349	    ${cfg.extraConfig}
   350	  '';
   351	
   352	  zmqServerEnabled = (cfg.zmqpubrawblock != null) || (cfg.zmqpubrawtx != null);
   353	
   354	  intAtLeast = n: types.addCheck types.int (x: x >= n) // {
   355	    name = "intAtLeast";
   356	    description = "integer >= ${toString n}";
   357	  };
   358	in {
   359	  inherit options;
   360	
   361	  config = mkIf cfg.enable {
   362	    environment.systemPackages = [ cfg.package (hiPrio cfg.cli) ];
   363	
   364	    services.bitcoind = mkMerge [
   365	      (mkIf cfg.dataDirReadableByGroup {
   366	        disablewallet = true;
   367	        sysperms = true;
   368	      })
   369	      {
   370	        rpc.users.privileged = {
   371	          passwordHMACFromFile = true;
   372	        };
   373	        rpc.users.public = {
   374	          passwordHMACFromFile = true;
   375	          # mkDefault so users can extend/replace the whitelist in their own config
   376	          rpcwhitelist = mkDefault (import ./bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix);
   377	        };
   378	      }
   379	    ];
   380	
   381	    services.i2pd = mkIf (cfg.i2p != false) {
   382	      enable = true;
   383	      proto.sam.enable = true;
   384	    };
   385	
   386	    systemd.tmpfiles.rules = [
   387	      "d '${cfg.dataDir}' 0770 ${cfg.user} ${cfg.group} - -"
   388	    ];
   389	
   390	    systemd.services.bitcoind = rec {
   391	      wants = [
   392	        "network-online.target"
   393	        # Use `wants` instead of `requires` for `nix-bitcoin-secrets.target`
   394	        # so that bitcoind and all dependent services are not restarted when
   395	        # the secrets target restarts.
   396	        # The secrets target always restarts when deploying with one of the methods
   397	        # in ./deployment.
   398	        #
   399	        # TODO-EXTERNAL: Instead of `wants`, use a future systemd dependency type
   400	        # that propagates initial start failures but no restarts
   401	        "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target"
   402	      ];
   403	      after = wants;
   404	      wantedBy = [ "multi-user.target" ];
   405	
   406	      preStart = let
   407	        extraRpcauth = concatMapStrings (name: let
   408	          user = cfg.rpc.users.${name};
   409	        in optionalString user.passwordHMACFromFile ''
   410	            echo "rpcauth=${user.name}:$(cat ${secretsDir}/bitcoin-HMAC-${name})"
   411	          ''
   412	        ) (builtins.attrNames cfg.rpc.users);
   413	      in ''
   414	        ${optionalString cfg.dataDirReadableByGroup ''
   415	          if [[ -e '${cfg.dataDir}/blocks' ]]; then
   416	            chmod -R g+rX '${cfg.dataDir}/blocks'
   417	          fi
   418	        ''}
   419	
   420	        cfg=$(
   421	          cat ${configFile}
   422	          ${extraRpcauth}
   423	          echo
   424	          ${optionalString (cfg.getPublicAddressCmd != "") ''
   425	            echo "externalip=$(${cfg.getPublicAddressCmd})"
   426	          ''}
   427	        )
   428	        confFile='${cfg.dataDir}/bitcoin.conf'
   429	        if [[ ! -e $confFile || $cfg != $(cat $confFile) ]]; then
   430	          install -o '${cfg.user}' -g '${cfg.group}' -m 640 <(echo "$cfg") $confFile
   431	        fi
   432	      '';
   433	
   434	      # Enable RPC access for group
   435	      postStart = ''
   436	        # NOTE: this makes the RPC cookie readable by group `bitcoin` — every
   437	        # service in that group gets full privileged RPC. Keep group membership
   438	        # minimal; new services should use an rpc user + HMAC instead (audit L-10).
   439	        # Use an explicit 0640 rather than `chmod g=r`: the latter leaves the
   440	        # other-read bit at whatever bitcoind created (its umask), so the cookie's
   441	        # world-readability would hinge on the app's file mode. 0640 clears it.
   442	        chmod 0640 '${cfg.dataDir}/${optionalString cfg.regtest "regtest/"}.cookie'
   443	      '' + (optionalString cfg.regtest) ''
   444	        chmod g=x '${cfg.dataDir}/regtest'
   445	      '';
   446	
   447	      serviceConfig = nbLib.defaultHardening // {
   448	        Type = "notify";
   449	        NotifyAccess = "all";
   450	        User = cfg.user;
   451	        Group = cfg.group;
   452	        TimeoutStartSec = "30min";
   453	        TimeoutStopSec = "30min";
   454	        ExecStart = "${cfg.package}/bin/bitcoind -datadir='${cfg.dataDir}'";
   455	        Restart = "on-failure";
   456	        UMask = mkIf cfg.dataDirReadableByGroup "0027";
   457	        ReadWritePaths = [ cfg.dataDir ];
   458	      } // nbLib.allowedIPAddresses cfg.tor.enforce
   459	        // optionalAttrs zmqServerEnabled nbLib.allowNetlink;
   460	    };
   461	
   462	    users.users.${cfg.user} = {
   463	      isSystemUser = true;
   464	      group = cfg.group;
   465	    };
   466	    users.groups.${cfg.group} = {};
   467	    users.groups.bitcoinrpc-public = {};
   468	
   469	    nix-bitcoin.operator.groups = [ cfg.group ];
   470	
   471	    nix-bitcoin.secrets = {
   472	      bitcoin-rpcpassword-privileged.user = cfg.user;
   473	      bitcoin-rpcpassword-public = {
   474	        user = cfg.user;
   475	        group = "bitcoinrpc-public";
   476	      };
   477	
   478	      bitcoin-HMAC-privileged.user = cfg.user;
   479	      bitcoin-HMAC-public.user = cfg.user;
   480	    };
   481	    nix-bitcoin.generateSecretsCmds.bitcoind = ''
   482	      makeBitcoinRPCPassword privileged
   483	      makeBitcoinRPCPassword public
   484	    '';
   485	  };
   486	}
     1	# RPC calls that are safe for public use.
     2	#
     3	# Safety bar: bounded CPU cost per call, no P2P side effects beyond
     4	# transaction relay, no leaks of peer topology or wallet contents.
     5	#
     6	# Deliberately excluded (audit M-3):
     7	# - scantxoutset, gettxoutsetinfo, getblocktemplate: minutes-scale CPU,
     8	#   trivially abused for DoS by any holder of the public credentials.
     9	# - getblockfrompeer: lets the caller drive the node's P2P block fetching.
    10	# - fundrawtransaction: operates on a loaded bitcoind wallet — it selects and
    11	#   locks UTXOs and reveals change/wallet contents, violating the wallet-safety
    12	#   bar above. No local consumer (lnd, electrs, nbxplorer) uses it; lnd/nbxplorer
    13	#   manage their own wallets and electrs only reads blocks. Removed in the audit
    14	#   follow-up 2026-08-20.
    15	# If you need any of these, grant them via
    16	# `services.bitcoind.rpc.users.public.rpcwhitelist` in your own config.
    17	#
    18	# NOTE: getpeerinfo / getnodeaddresses stay in the list. This "public" user
    19	# is also the default RPC user that LOCAL services use, and lnd REQUIRES both
    20	# (its chain-backend health check + pruned-mode block fetching) — removing
    21	# them makes lnd fail its health check and shut itself down. They only leak
    22	# peer topology if you actually expose this user over a public proxy; if you
    23	# do that, narrow the whitelist in your own config rather than here.
    24	[
    25	  "echo"
    26	  "getinfo"
    27	  "getindexinfo"
    28	  "help"
    29	  "ping"
    30	  "uptime"
    31	  # Blockchain
    32	  "getbestblockhash"
    33	  "getblock"
    34	  "getblockchaininfo"
    35	  "getblockcount"
    36	  "getblockfilter"
    37	  "getblockhash"
    38	  "getblockheader"
    39	  "getblockstats"
    40	  "getchaintips"
    41	  "getchaintxstats"
    42	  "getdeploymentinfo"
    43	  "getdifficulty"
    44	  "getmempoolancestors"
    45	  "getmempooldescendants"
    46	  "getmempoolentry"
    47	  "getmempoolinfo"
    48	  "getrawmempool"
    49	  "gettxout"
    50	  "gettxoutproof"
    51	  "verifytxoutproof"
    52	  # Mining
    53	  "getmininginfo"
    54	  "getnetworkhashps"
    55	  # Network
    56	  "getnetworkinfo"
    57	  "getnodeaddresses"  # lnd (pruned block fetching)
    58	  "getpeerinfo"       # lnd (chain-backend health check), nbxplorer
    59	  # Rawtransactions
    60	  "analyzepsbt"
    61	  "combinepsbt"
    62	  "combinerawtransaction"
    63	  "converttopsbt"
    64	  "createpsbt"
    65	  "createrawtransaction"
    66	  "decodepsbt"
    67	  "decoderawtransaction"
    68	  "decodescript"
    69	  "finalizepsbt"
    70	  "getrawtransaction"
    71	  "joinpsbts"
    72	  "sendrawtransaction"
    73	  "signrawtransactionwithkey"
    74	  "testmempoolaccept"
    75	  "utxoupdatepsbt"
    76	  # Util
    77	  "createmultisig"
    78	  "deriveaddresses"
    79	  "estimatesmartfee"
    80	  "getdescriptorinfo"
    81	  "signmessagewithprivkey"
    82	  "validateaddress"
    83	  "verifymessage"
    84	  # Zmq
    85	  "getzmqnotifications"
    86	]

     3	with lib;
     4	let
     5	  options.nix-bitcoin.netns-isolation = {
     6	    enable = mkEnableOption "netns isolation";
     7	
     8	    addressblock = mkOption {
     9	      type = types.ints.u8;
    10	      default = 1;
    11	      description = ''
    12	        The address block N in 169.254.N.0/24, used as the prefix for netns addresses.
    13	      '';
    14	    };
    15	
    16	    services = mkOption {
    17	      default = {};
    18	      type = types.attrsOf (types.submodule {
    19	        options = {
    20	          id = mkOption {
    21	            # TODO: Assert uniqueness
    22	            type = types.ints.between 11 255;
    23	            description = ''
    24	              id for the netns, used for the IP address host part and
    25	              for naming the interfaces. Must be unique. Must be greater than 10.
    26	            '';
    27	          };
    28	          connections = mkOption {
    29	            type = with types; listOf str;
    30	            default = [];
    31	          };
    32	        };
    33	      });
    34	    };
    35	
    36	    allowedUser = mkOption {
    37	      type = types.str;
    38	      description = ''
    39	        User that is allowed to execute commands in the service network namespaces.
    40	      '';
    41	      default = config.nix-bitcoin.operator.name;
    42	    };
    43	
    44	    netns = mkOption {
    45	      readOnly = true;
    46	      default = netns;
    47	      description = "Exposes netns parameters.";
    48	    };
    49	
    50	    bridgeIp = mkOption {
    51	      readOnly = true;
    52	      default = bridgeIp;
    53	      description = "IP of the netns bridge interface.";
    54	    };
    55	  };
    56	
    57	  cfg = config.nix-bitcoin.netns-isolation;
    58	
    59	  netns = builtins.mapAttrs (n: v: {
    60	    inherit (v) id;
    61	    address = "169.254.${toString cfg.addressblock}.${toString v.id}";
    62	    availableNetns = availableNetns.${n};
    63	    netnsName = "nb-${n}";
    64	  }) enabledServices;
    65	
    66	  # Symmetric netns connection matrix
    67	  # if clightning.connections = [ "bitcoind" ]; then
    68	  #   availableNetns.bitcoind = [ "clighting" ];
    69	  #   and
    70	  #   availableNetns.clighting = [ "bitcoind" ];
    71	  #
    72	  # TODO-EXTERNAL:
    73	  # Although negligible for our purposes, this calculation's runtime
    74	  # is in the order of (number of connections * number of services),
    75	  # because attrsets and lists are fully copied on each update with '//' or '++'.
    76	  # This can only be improved with an update in the nix language.
    77	  #
    78	  availableNetns = let
    79	    # base = { clightning = [ "bitcoind" ]; ... }
    80	    base = builtins.mapAttrs (n: v:
    81	      builtins.filter isEnabled v.connections
    82	    ) enabledServices;
    83	  in
    84	    foldl (xs: s1:
    85	      foldl (xs: s2:
    86	        xs // { "${s2}" = xs.${s2} ++ [ s1 ]; }
    87	      ) xs cfg.services.${s1}.connections
    88	    ) base (builtins.attrNames base);
    89	
    90	  enabledServices = filterAttrs (n: v: isEnabled n) cfg.services;
    91	  isEnabled = x: config.services.${x}.enable;
    92	
    93	  ip = "${pkgs.iproute2}/bin/ip";
    94	  iptables = "${config.networking.firewall.package}/bin/iptables";
    95	
    96	  bridgeIp = "169.254.${toString cfg.addressblock}.10";
    97	in {
    98	  inherit options;

   101	  inherit (config.services) bitcoind;
   102	  # Neither NBXplorer nor BTCPay ships an appsettings*.json; their settings
   103	  # come from the config file we write (loaded without reload). So the generic
   104	  # host's reload-on-change watcher has nothing to watch and would only ever
   105	  # walk the content root recursively. Turn it off.
   106	  dotnetNoReload = { DOTNET_hostBuilder__reloadConfigOnChange = "false"; };
   107	in {
   108	  inherit options;
   109	
   110	  config = mkIf cfg.btcpayserver.enable {
   111	    services.bitcoind = {
   112	      enable = true;
   113	      rpc.users.btcpayserver = {
   114	        passwordHMACFromFile = true;
   115	        # nbxplorer/btcpayserver need only the read/relay calls in the public
   116	        # whitelist. The former `setban` + `generatetoaddress` extras were
   117	        # over-grants for the RPC user reachable from the internet-facing
   118	        # btcpayserver: setban lets a web-app compromise eclipse the node
   119	        # (persistent peer bans), and generatetoaddress burns CPU via `maxtries`
   120	        # on mainnet and is regtest-only otherwise. Neither is used by nbxplorer,
   121	        # btcpayserver, or the test suite. Removed in the audit follow-up
   122	        # 2026-08-20; if you run btcpay in regtest and want its "mine" button,
   123	        # grant generatetoaddress in your own config.
   124	        rpcwhitelist = cfg.bitcoind.rpc.users.public.rpcwhitelist;
   125	        # Note: nbxplorer's Indexer.ConnectNode needs getpeerinfo — it is in the
   126	        # public whitelist (which this inherits), because lnd needs it too. See
   127	        # modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix.
   128	      };
   129	      listenWhitelisted = true;
   130	    };
   131	    services.lnd = mkIf (cfg.btcpayserver.lightningBackend == "lnd") {
   132	      enable = true;
   133	      macaroons.btcpayserver = {
   134	        inherit (cfg.btcpayserver) user;
   135	        permissions = ''{"entity":"info","action":"read"},{"entity":"onchain","action":"read"},{"entity":"offchain","action":"read"},{"entity":"address","action":"read"},{"entity":"message","action":"read"},{"entity":"peers","action":"read"},{"entity":"signer","action":"read"},{"entity":"invoices","action":"read"},{"entity":"invoices","action":"write"},{"entity":"address","action":"write"}'';
   136	      };
   137	    };
   138	    services.postgresql = {
   139	      enable = true;
   140	      ensureDatabases = [
   141	        "btcpaydb" # This name is kept for backwards compatibility
   142	        "nbxplorer"
   143	      ];
   144	      ensureUsers = [
   145	        { name = cfg.btcpayserver.user; }
   146	        { name = cfg.nbxplorer.user; }
   147	      ];
   148	    };
   149	    systemd.services.postgresql-setup.postStart = ''
   150	      psql -tAc '
   151	        ALTER DATABASE "btcpaydb" OWNER TO "${cfg.btcpayserver.user}";
   152	        ALTER DATABASE "nbxplorer" OWNER TO "${cfg.nbxplorer.user}";
   153	      '
   154	    '';
   155	
   156	    systemd.tmpfiles.rules = [
   157	      "d '${cfg.nbxplorer.dataDir}' 0770 ${cfg.nbxplorer.user} ${cfg.nbxplorer.group} - -"
   158	      "d '${cfg.btcpayserver.dataDir}' 0770 ${cfg.btcpayserver.user} ${cfg.btcpayserver.group} - -"
   159	    ];

   161	    systemd.services.nbxplorer = let
   162	      configFile = builtins.toFile "config" ''
   163	        network=${bitcoind.network}
   164	        btcrpcuser=${cfg.bitcoind.rpc.users.btcpayserver.name}
   165	        btcrpcurl=http://${nbLib.addressWithPort bitcoind.rpc.address cfg.bitcoind.rpc.port}
   166	        btcnodeendpoint=${nbLib.addressWithPort bitcoind.address bitcoind.whitelistedPort}
   167	        bind=${cfg.nbxplorer.address}
   168	        port=${toString cfg.nbxplorer.port}
   169	        postgres=User ID=${cfg.nbxplorer.user};Host=/run/postgresql;Database=nbxplorer
   170	      '';
   171	    in rec {
   172	      wantedBy = [ "multi-user.target" ];
   173	      requires = [ "postgresql.target" ];
   174	      wants = [ "bitcoind.service" ];
   175	      after = requires ++ wants ++ [ "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target" ];
   176	      environment = dotnetNoReload;
   177	      preStart = ''
   178	        install -m 600 ${configFile} '${cfg.nbxplorer.dataDir}/settings.config'
   179	        {
   180	          echo "btcrpcpassword=$(cat ${config.nix-bitcoin.secretsDir}/bitcoin-rpcpassword-btcpayserver)"
   181	        } >> '${cfg.nbxplorer.dataDir}/settings.config'
   182	      '';
   183	      serviceConfig = nbLib.defaultHardening // {
   184	        ExecStart = ''
   185	          ${cfg.nbxplorer.package}/bin/nbxplorer --conf=${cfg.nbxplorer.dataDir}/settings.config \
   186	            --datadir=${cfg.nbxplorer.dataDir}
   187	        '';
   188	        User = cfg.nbxplorer.user;
   189	        # The .NET host takes its ASP.NET content root from the working
   190	        # directory. Without an explicit one that root was `/`, and the host's
   191	        # reload-on-change watcher registered an inotify watch on every
   192	        # directory of the root filesystem -- the whole Nix store -- plus one
   193	        # for each directory created afterwards. On a node that builds, this
   194	        # exhausted the per-user inotify watch limit within days and systemd
   195	        # then failed to set up cgroup accounting for every new unit.
   196	        # Use the immutable app directory (as btcpayserver does below), NOT the
   197	        # data directory: a writable content root would let anything running
   198	        # as the nbxplorer user drop an `appsettings.json` that overrides the
   199	        # Nix-managed settings.config on the next start.
   200	        WorkingDirectory = "${cfg.nbxplorer.package}/lib/nbxplorer";
   201	        Restart = "on-failure";
   202	        RestartSec = "10s";
   203	        ReadWritePaths = [ cfg.nbxplorer.dataDir ];
   204	        # .NET's JIT needs writable+executable pages, so W^X enforcement cannot
   205	        # be enabled for NBXplorer or BTCPay. Accepted trade-off (audit
   206	        # 2026-08-29, low): the remaining sandbox (nbLib.defaultHardening,
   207	        # netns/Tor policy, dedicated users) is unchanged.
   208	        MemoryDenyWriteExecute = false;
   209	      } // nbLib.allowedIPAddresses cfg.nbxplorer.tor.enforce;
   210	    };
   211	
   212	    systemd.services.btcpayserver = let
   213	      nbExplorerUrl = "http://${nbLib.addressWithPort cfg.nbxplorer.address cfg.nbxplorer.port}/";

   239	      after = requires ++ wants;

   243	            --datadir='${cfg.btcpayserver.dataDir}'
   244	        '';

   246	        # Since 2.4.0, btcpayserver uses `Host.CreateDefaultBuilder`, which sets the
   247	        # ASP.NET content root to the working directory instead of the app directory.
   248	        # The web root (`wwwroot`) is resolved relative to the content root, so
   249	        # btcpayserver must be started from its app directory.
   250	        WorkingDirectory = "${cfg.btcpayserver.package}/lib/btcpayserver";

   252	        # This is required to support restarting from the web interface after
   253	        # interactive plugin installation.

   255	        Restart = "always";

   258	      } // nbLib.allowedIPAddresses cfg.btcpayserver.tor.enforce;

   260	      startLimitBurst = 10;
   261	    };

     5	  options.services.electrs = {
     6	    enable = mkEnableOption "electrs, an Electrum server implemented in Rust";
     7	    address = mkOption {
     8	      type = types.str;
     9	      default = "127.0.0.1";
    10	      description = "Address to listen for RPC connections.";
    11	    };

    16	    };

    18	      type = types.path;
    19	      default = "/var/lib/electrs";
    20	      description = "The data directory for electrs.";
    21	    };
    22	    monitoringPort = mkOption {
    23	      type = types.port;
    24	      default = 4224;
    25	      description = "Prometheus monitoring port.";
    26	    };
    27	    extraArgs = mkOption {
    28	      type = types.separatedString " ";
    29	      default = "";
    30	      description = "Extra command line arguments passed to electrs.";
    31	    };
    32	    user = mkOption {
    33	      type = types.str;
    34	      default = "electrs";
    35	      description = "The user as which to run electrs.";
    36	    };
    37	    group = mkOption {
    38	      type = types.str;
    39	      default = cfg.user;
    40	      description = "The group as which to run electrs.";
    41	    };
    42	    tor.enforce = nbLib.tor.enforce;
    43	  };
    44	
    45	  cfg = config.services.electrs;
    46	  nbLib = config.nix-bitcoin.lib;
    47	  secretsDir = config.nix-bitcoin.secretsDir;
    48	  bitcoind = config.services.bitcoind;
    49	in {
    50	  inherit options;
    51	
    52	  config = mkIf cfg.enable {
    53	    assertions = [
    54	      { assertion = bitcoind.prune == 0;
    55	        message = "electrs does not support bitcoind pruning.";
    56	      }
    57	    ];
    58	
    59	    services.bitcoind = {
    60	      enable = true;
    61	      listenWhitelisted = true;
    62	    };
    63	
    64	    systemd.tmpfiles.rules = [
    65	      "d '${cfg.dataDir}' 0770 ${cfg.user} ${cfg.group} - -"
    66	    ];
    67	
    68	    systemd.services.electrs = {
    69	      wantedBy = [ "multi-user.target" ];
    70	      # `wants`, not `requires`: electrs tolerates its bitcoind backend going
    71	      # away and reconnects on its own. A hard `requires` meant every bitcoind
    72	      # restart cascade-stopped electrs, and `nixos-rebuild switch` did not
    73	      # bring it back — Electrum clients stayed cut off until a manual start.
    74	      # `after` still orders electrs behind bitcoind at boot; `wants` pulls
    75	      # bitcoind in without the cascade-stop. Mirrors the lnd fix (8aa7f5e).
    76	      wants = [ "bitcoind.service" ];
    77	      after = [ "bitcoind.service" "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target" ];
    78	      preStart = ''
    79	        # install -m 600 (not a bare `>`): electrs.toml holds the public RPC
    80	        # password, and the 0770 datadir must not be the only thing protecting
    81	        # it. Matches lnd.conf / nbxplorer settings.config.
    82	        install -m 600 \
    83	          <(echo "auth = \"${bitcoind.rpc.users.public.name}:$(cat ${secretsDir}/bitcoin-rpcpassword-public)\"") \
    84	          electrs.toml
    85	        '';
    86	      serviceConfig = nbLib.defaultHardening // {
    87	        # electrs only uses the working directory for reading electrs.toml
    88	        WorkingDirectory = cfg.dataDir;
    89	        ExecStart = ''
    90	          ${config.nix-bitcoin.pkgs.electrs}/bin/electrs \
    91	          --log-filters=INFO \
    92	          --network=${bitcoind.makeNetworkName "bitcoin" "regtest"} \
    93	          --db-dir='${cfg.dataDir}' \
    94	          --daemon-dir='${bitcoind.dataDir}' \
    95	          --electrum-rpc-addr=${cfg.address}:${toString cfg.port} \
    96	          --monitoring-addr=${cfg.address}:${toString cfg.monitoringPort} \
    97	          --daemon-rpc-addr=${nbLib.addressWithPort bitcoind.rpc.address bitcoind.rpc.port} \
    98	          --daemon-p2p-addr=${nbLib.addressWithPort bitcoind.address bitcoind.whitelistedPort} \
    99	          ${cfg.extraArgs}
   100	        '';
   101	        User = cfg.user;
   102	        Group = cfg.group;
   103	        Restart = "on-failure";
   104	        RestartSec = "10s";
   105	        ReadWritePaths = [ cfg.dataDir ];
   106	      } // nbLib.allowedIPAddresses cfg.tor.enforce;
   107	    };
   108	
   109	    users.users.${cfg.user} = {
   110	      isSystemUser = true;
   111	      group = cfg.group;
   112	      extraGroups = [ "bitcoinrpc-public" ];
   113	    };
   114	    users.groups.${cfg.group} = {};
   115	  };
   116	}

codex
The password-argv and onion-hostname injection fixes still hold in the code I’ve read. I’m now checking two less obvious boundaries: whether the “safe” RPC methods can still exhaust resources, and whether WireGuard or namespace option combinations bypass the intended filtering. I’m also tracing the root macaroon helper’s reads and writes.

     1	{ modulesPath, ... }: {

     5	    (modulesPath + "/profiles/hardened.nix")

     9

     1	# This preset adds additional hardening settings on top of the
     2	# default ./hardened.nix preset.
     3	# These settings trade even more functionality and performance for increased security.
     4	
     5	# This preset enables usbguard. Use `services.usbguard.rules` to whitelist
     6	# select devices.
     7	#
     8	# See madaidan's Linux Hardening Guide for detailed explanations:
     9	# https://madaidans-insecurities.github.io/guides/linux-hardening.html
    10	
    11	{
    12	  imports = [
    13	    # Build on standard hardened preset
    14	    ./hardened.nix
    15	  ];
    16	
    17	  boot.kernel.sysctl = {
    18	    # Prevent boot console kernel log information leaks
    19	    "kernel.printk" = "3 3 3 3";
    20	
    21	    # Restrict loading TTY line disciplines to the CAP_SYS_MODULE capability to
    22	    # prevent unprivileged attackers from loading vulnerable line disciplines with
    23	    # the TIOCSETD ioctl
    24	    "dev.tty.ldisc_autoload" = "0";
    25	
    26	    # The SysRq key exposes a lot of potentially dangerous debugging functionality
    27	    # to unprivileged users
    28	    "kernel.sysrq" = "4";
    29	
    30	    # Protect against time-wait assassination by dropping RST packets for sockets
    31	    # in the time-wait state
    32	    "net.ipv4.tcp_rfc1337" = "1";
    33	
    34	    # Disable accepting IPv6 router advertisements
    35	    "net.ipv6.conf.all.accept_ra" = "0";
    36	    "net.ipv6.default.accept_ra" = "0";
    37	
    38	    # Disable TCP SACK. SACK is commonly exploited and unnecessary for many
    39	    # circumstances so it should be disabled if you don't require it
    40	    "net.ipv4.tcp_sack" = "0";
    41	    "net.ipv4.tcp_dsack" = "0";
    42	
    43	    # Restrict usage of ptrace to only processes with the CAP_SYS_PTRACE
    44	    # capability
    45	    "kernel.yama.ptrace_scope" = "2";
    46	
    47	    # Prevent creating files in potentially attacker-controlled environments such
    48	    # as world-writable directories to make data spoofing attacks more difficult
    49	    "fs.protected_fifos" = "2";
    50	    "fs.protected_regular" = "2";
    51	
    52	    # Avoid leaking system time with TCP timestamps
    53	    "net.ipv4.tcp_timestamps" = "0";
    54	
    55	    # Disable core dumps
    56	    "kernel.core_pattern" = "|/bin/false";
    57	    "fs.suid_dumpable" = "0";
    58	
    59	    # Only swap when absolutely necessary
    60	    "vm.swappiness" = "1";
    61	  };
    62	
    63	  boot.kernelParams = [
    64	    # Disable slab merging which significantly increases the difficulty of heap
    65	    # exploitation by preventing overwriting objects from merged caches and by
    66	    # making it harder to influence slab cache layout
    67	    "slab_nomerge"
    68	
    69	    # Disable vsyscalls as they are obsolete and have been replaced with vDSO.
    70	    # vsyscalls are also at fixed addresses in memory, making them a potential
    71	    # target for ROP attacks
    72	    "vsyscall=none"
    73	
    74	    # Disable debugfs which exposes a lot of sensitive information about the
    75	    # kernel
    76	    "debugfs=off"
    77	
    78	    # Sometimes certain kernel exploits will cause what is known as an "oops".
    79	    # This parameter will cause the kernel to panic on such oopses, thereby
    80	    # preventing those exploits
    81	    "oops=panic"
    82	
    83	    # Only allow kernel modules that have been signed with a valid key to be
    84	    # loaded, which increases security by making it much harder to load a
    85	    # malicious kernel module
    86	    "module.sig_enforce=1"
    87	
    88	    # The kernel lockdown LSM can eliminate many methods that user space code
    89	    # could abuse to escalate to kernel privileges and extract sensitive
    90	    # information. This LSM is necessary to implement a clear security boundary
    91	    # between user space and the kernel
    92	    "lockdown=confidentiality"
    93

     9	    # Without this, bitcoin-cli would try to use the .cookie file in the local
    10	    # bitcoind data dir for authorization, which doesn't exist.

    13	    '';

    17	    preStart = lib.mkAfter ''
    18	      # Rewrite, don't append: the old version added a duplicate line on every
    19	      # restart (audit L-11). The privileged RPC password must stay 0640
    20	      # (owner bitcoin, group bitcoin) so the operator can read it for
    21	      # bitcoin-cli, but not world-readable.
    22	      conf='${cfg.dataDir}/bitcoin.conf'
    23	      ${pkgs.gnused}/bin/sed -i '/^rpcpassword=/d' "$conf"
    24	      echo "rpcpassword=$(cat ${secretsDir}/bitcoin-rpcpassword-privileged)" >> "$conf"
    25	      chmod 0640 "$conf"
    26	    '';
    27	    postStart = lib.mkForce "";

    29	      Type = lib.mkForce "oneshot";
    30	      ExecStart = lib.mkForce "${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/true";
    31	      RemainAfterExit = true;
    32	    };

     1	{ lib, ... }:
     2	{
     3	  nix-bitcoin = {
     4	    secretsDir = "/var/src/secrets";
     5	    setupSecrets = true;
     6	  };
     7	  environment.variables.NIX_PATH = lib.mkForce "/var/src";
     8	
     9	  # The file syncing step in krops resets the secrets file permissions.
    10	  # So force `setup-secrets.service` to restart on deployment.
    11	  # Stop it at activation start so that it gets restarted at the end.
    12	  system.activationScripts.nixBitcoinStopSetupSecrets = ''
    13	    ${/* Skip this step if systemd is not running, i.e. when booting or in nixos-install */ ""}
    14	    if [[ -e /run/systemd/system ]]; then
    15	      if ! output=$(/run/current-system/systemd/bin/systemctl stop setup-secrets.service --no-block 2>&1); then
    16	        # Ignore if the unit is not loaded, which can happen on the first deployment
    17	        if [[ $output != *setup-secrets.service\ not\ loaded* ]]; then
    18	          echo "$output"
    19	          false
    20	        fi
    21	      fi
    22	    fi
    23	  '';
    24	}
     1	{ config, ... }:
     2	{
     3	  nix-bitcoin.secretsSetupMethod = "nixops";
     4	
     5	  deployment.keys = builtins.mapAttrs (n: v: {
     6	    keyFile = "${config.nix-bitcoin.deployment.secretsDir}/${n}";
     7	    destDir = config.nix-bitcoin.secretsDir;
     8	    inherit (v) user group permissions;
     9	  }) config.nix-bitcoin.secrets;
    10	
    11	  # nixops makes the secrets directory accessible only for users with group 'key'.
    12	  # For compatibility with other deployment methods besides nixops, we forego the
    13	  # use of the 'key' group and make the secrets dir world-readable instead.
    14	  # This is safe because all containing files have their specific private
    15	  # permissions set.
    16	  systemd.services.allowSecretsDirAccess = {
    17	    requires = [ "keys.target" ];
    18	    after = [ "keys.target" ];
    19	    script = "chmod o+x ${config.nix-bitcoin.secretsDir}";
    20	    serviceConfig.Type = "oneshot";
    21	  };
    22	
    23	  systemd.targets.nix-bitcoin-secrets = {
    24	    requires = [ "allowSecretsDirAccess.service" ];
    25	    after = [ "allowSecretsDirAccess.service" ];
    26	  };
    27	}
     1	{ config, lib, pkgs, ... }:
     2	
     3	with lib;
     4	let
     5	  options = {
     6	    nix-bitcoin.nodeinfo = {
     7	      enable = mkEnableOption "nodeinfo";
     8	
     9	      program = mkOption {
    10	        readOnly = true;
    11	        default = script;
    12	        defaultText = "(See source)";
    13	      };
    14	
    15	      services = mkOption {
    16	        internal = true;
    17	        type = types.attrs;
    18	        default = {};
    19	        defaultText = "(See source)";
    20	        description = ''
    21	          Nodeinfo service definitions.
    22	        '';
    23	      };
    24	
    25	      lib = mkOption {
    26	        internal = true;
    27	        readOnly = true;
    28	        default = nodeinfoLib;
    29	        defaultText = "(See source)";
    30	        description = ''
    31	          Helper functions for defining nodeinfo services.
    32	        '';
    33	      };
    34	    };
    35	  };
    36	
    37	  cfg = config.nix-bitcoin.nodeinfo;
    38	  nbLib = config.nix-bitcoin.lib;
    39	
    40	  script = pkgs.writeScriptBin "nodeinfo" ''
    41	    #!${pkgs.python3}/bin/python
    42	
    43	    import json
    44	    import subprocess
    45	    import sys
    46	    from collections import OrderedDict
    47	
    48	    def success(*args):
    49	        return subprocess.call(args, stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL) == 0
    50	
    51	    def is_active(unit):
    52	        return success("systemctl", "is-active", "--quiet", unit)
    53	
    54	    def is_enabled(unit):
    55	        return success("systemctl", "is-enabled", "--quiet", unit)
    56	
    57	    def cmd(*args):
    58	        return subprocess.run(args, stdout=subprocess.PIPE).stdout.decode('utf-8')
    59	
    60	    def shell(*args):
    61	        return cmd("bash", "-c", *args).strip()
    62	
    63	    infos = OrderedDict()
    64	    operator = "${config.nix-bitcoin.operator.name}"
    65	
    66	    def get_onion_address(name, port):
    67	        path = f"/var/lib/onion-addresses/{operator}/{name}"
    68	        try:
    69	            with open(path, "r") as f:
    70	                onion_address = f.read().strip()
    71	        except OSError:
    72	            print(f"error reading file {path}", file=sys.stderr)
    73	            return
    74	        return f"{onion_address}:{port}"
    75	
    76	    def add_service(service, make_info, systemd_service = None):
    77	        systemd_service = systemd_service or service
    78	        if not is_active(systemd_service):
    79	            infos[service] = f"'{systemd_service}.service' is not running"
    80	        else:
    81	            info = OrderedDict()
    82	            exec(make_info, globals(), locals())
    83	            infos[service] = info
    84	
    85	    if is_enabled("onion-addresses") and not is_active("onion-addresses"):
    86	        print("error: service 'onion-addresses' is not running")
    87	        exit(1)
    88	
    89	    ${concatStrings infos}
    90	
    91	    print(json.dumps(infos, indent=2))
    92	  '';
    93	
    94	  infos = map (serviceName:
    95	    let serviceCfg = config.services.${serviceName};
    96	    in optionalString serviceCfg.enable (cfg.services.${serviceName} serviceName serviceCfg)
    97	  ) (builtins.attrNames cfg.services);
    98	
    99	  nodeinfoLib = rec {
   100	    mkInfo = extraCode: name: cfg:
   101	      mkInfoLong {
   102	        inherit extraCode name cfg;
   103	      };
   104	
   105	    mkInfoLong = { extraCode ? "", name, cfg, systemdServiceName ? name }: ''
   106	      add_service("${name}", """
   107	      info["local_address"] = "${nbLib.addressWithPort cfg.address cfg.port}"
   108	    '' + mkIfOnionPort name (onionPort: ''
   109	      info["onion_address"] = get_onion_address("${name}", ${onionPort})
   110	    '') + extraCode + ''
   111	
   112	      """, "${systemdServiceName}")
   113	    '';
   114	
   115	    mkIfOnionPort = name: fn:
   116	      if onionServices ? ${name} then
   117	        fn (toString (builtins.elemAt onionServices.${name}.map 0).port)
   118	      else
   119	        "";
   120	  };
   121	
   122	  inherit (config.services.tor.relay) onionServices;
   123	in {
   124	  inherit options;
   125	
   126	  config = mkIf cfg.enable {
   127	    environment.systemPackages = [ script ];
   128	
   129	    nix-bitcoin.operator.enable = true;
   130	
   131	    nix-bitcoin.nodeinfo.services = with nodeinfoLib; {
   132	      bitcoind = mkInfo "";
   133	      lnd = name: cfg: mkInfo (''
   134	        info["rest_address"] = "${nbLib.addressWithPort cfg.restAddress cfg.restPort}"
   135	      '' + mkIfOnionPort "lnd-rest" (onionPort: ''
   136	        info["onion_rest_address"] = get_onion_address("lnd-rest", ${onionPort})
   137	      '') + ''
   138	        info["nodeid"] = shell("lncli getinfo | jq -r '.identity_pubkey'")
   139	      '') name cfg;
   140	      electrs = mkInfo "";
   141	      btcpayserver = mkInfo "";
   142	      # Only add sshd when it has an onion service
   143	      sshd = name: cfg: mkIfOnionPort "sshd" (onionPort: ''
   144	        add_service("sshd", """info["onion_address"] = get_onion_address("sshd", ${onionPort})""")
   145	      '');
   146	    };
   147	  };
   148	}

     1	# Security-driven version overrides for packages whose pinned nixpkgs
     2	# version ships known vulnerabilities.
     3	#
     4	# Each override must be version-guarded so it turns inert once the pinned
     5	# nixpkgs catches up. Flake updates are then always safe to take.
     6	#
     7	# Example (from the clboss fund-theft fix, since resolved by
     8	# trimming clightning support from this fork):
     9	#
    10	#   clboss = if lagging pkgsUnstable.clboss "0.16.2" then pkgsUnstable.clboss.overrideAttrs (old: rec {
    11	#     version = "0.16.2";
    12	#     src = pkgs.fetchzip {
    13	#       url = "https://github.com/ZmnSCPxj/clboss/releases/download/v${version}/clboss-v${version}.tar.gz";
    14	#       hash = "...";
    15	#     };
    16	#   }) else pkgsUnstable.clboss;

    19	  inherit (pkgs) lib;
    20	  lagging = pkg: version: lib.versionOlder pkg.version version;
    21	in
    22	{
    23	  # 2.4.3 (2026-08-24) is a security release ("updating is recommended for
    24	  # servers shared with many users"); nixpkgs 26.05 carries 2.4.2. The nuget
    25	  # deps changed (HtmlSanitizer 9.1.982 -> 9.2.995 among others), so this is a

    28	  # together with this override once the pin catches up.

    30	                 then pkgs.callPackage ./btcpayserver { }

     1	/* This program requires CAP_SYS_ADMIN */
     2	
     3	#define _GNU_SOURCE
     4	#include <sched.h>
     5	#include <stdio.h>
     6	#include <errno.h>
     7	#include <string.h>
     8	#include <unistd.h>
     9	#include <fcntl.h>
    10	#include <sys/capability.h>
    11	
    12	/* Netns names of the services this fork ships (see modules/netns-isolation.nix) */
    13	static char *allowed_netns[] = {
    14	    "nb-bitcoind",
    15	    "nb-lnd",
    16	    "nb-electrs",
    17	    "nb-nbxplorer",
    18	    "nb-btcpayserver"
    19	};
    20	
    21	int is_netns_allowed(char *netns) {
    22	    int n_allowed_netns = sizeof(allowed_netns) / sizeof(allowed_netns[0]);
    23	    for (int i = 0; i < n_allowed_netns; i++) {
    24	        if (strcmp(allowed_netns[i], netns) == 0) {
    25	            return 1;
    26	        }
    27	    }
    28	    return 0;
    29	}
    30	
    31	void print_capabilities() {
    32	    cap_t caps = cap_get_proc();
    33	    printf("Capabilities: %s\n", cap_to_text(caps, NULL));
    34	    cap_free(caps);
    35	}
    36	
    37	int drop_capabilities() {
    38	    cap_t caps = cap_get_proc();
    39	    if (caps == NULL) return -1;
    40	    if (cap_clear(caps) < 0) { cap_free(caps); return -1; }
    41	    int rc = cap_set_proc(caps);
    42	    cap_free(caps);
    43	    return rc;
    44	}
    45	
    46	int main(int argc, char **argv) {
    47	    char netns_path[256];
    48	
    49	    if (argc < 3) {
    50	        printf("usage: %s <netns> <command>\n", argv[0]);
    51	        return 1;
    52	    }
    53	
    54	    if (!is_netns_allowed(argv[1])) {
    55	        printf("%s is not an allowed netns.\n", argv[1]);
    56	        return 1;
    57	    }
    58	
    59	    /* Require an absolute path so PATH cannot redirect the exec target.
    60	       The caller's environment is otherwise inherited; the operator who may
    61	       run this wrapper is already root-equivalent. */
    62	    if (argv[2][0] != '/') {
    63	        printf("command must be an absolute path.\n");
    64	        return 1;
    65	    }
    66	
    67	    int len = snprintf(netns_path, sizeof(netns_path), "/var/run/netns/%s", argv[1]);
    68	    if (len < 0 || (size_t)len >= sizeof(netns_path)) {
    69	        printf("Path length exceeded for netns %s.\n", argv[1]);
    70	        return 1;
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    int fd = open(netns_path, O_RDONLY);
    74	    if (fd < 0) {
    75	        printf("Failed opening netns %s: %d, %s \n", netns_path, errno, strerror(errno));
    76	        return 1;
    77	    }
    78	
    79	    if (setns(fd, CLONE_NEWNET) < 0) {
    80	        printf("Failed setns %d, %s \n", errno, strerror(errno));
    81	        close(fd);
    82	        return 1;
    83	    }
    84	    close(fd);
    85	
    86	    /* Drop capabilities */
    87	    #ifdef DEBUG
    88	    print_capabilities();
    89	    #endif
    90	    if (drop_capabilities() < 0) {
    91	        printf("Failed to drop capabilities: %d, %s \n", errno, strerror(errno));
    92	        return 1;
    93	    }
    94	    #ifdef DEBUG
    95	    print_capabilities();
    96	    #endif
    97	
    98	    execv(argv[2], &argv[2]);
    99	    /* Only reached on exec failure */
   100	    printf("Failed to exec %s: %d, %s \n", argv[2], errno, strerror(errno));
   101	    return 1;
   102	}
main: main.c

     9	  '';

       };
+      # This service runs as root with CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE/CAP_DAC_READ_SEARCH/
+      # CAP_CHOWN, so it must never follow an attacker-planted symlink: doing so
+      # would let a compromised service user redirect a read or a chown to an
+      # arbitrary path (e.g. copy a macaroon out, or take ownership of /etc/shadow).
+      # Two rules enforce this:
+      #   1. Refuse to read a Tor hostname file that is a symlink.
+      #   2. Build each output directory as root:root, fully populate it, and
+      #      only THEN hand ownership to the target user — so the user never has
+      #      write access to the directory while root is still writing into it,
+      #      and there is no window to race a symlink in. All chowns use `-h` so
+      #      a symlink is never dereferenced even if one somehow appears.
       script = ''
+        set -euo pipefail
+
         waitForFile() {
-          file=$1
+          local file=$1
           for ((i=0; i<300; i++)); do
             if [[ -e $file ]]; then
-              return;
+              return
             fi
             sleep 0.1
           done
-          echo "Error: File $file did not appear after 30 sec."
+          echo "Error: File $file did not appear after 30 sec." >&2
           exit 1
         }
 
+        # Copy a Tor hostname file, refusing to read it through a symlink and
+        # refusing to copy anything that is not exactly one v3 onion address.
+        #
+        # Content validation is load-bearing, not cosmetic: this file's bytes are
+        # spliced verbatim into bitcoin.conf/lnd.conf (`externalip=$(cat …)`) and
+        # into the lndconnect admin-macaroon URL (`--host=$(cat …)`). A tor-user
+        # who can write /var/lib/tor/onion/<svc>/hostname could otherwise plant a
+        # second line (`shutdownnotify=…`, `bitcoind.rpchost=…`) and get it
+        # executed / honoured downstream. Requiring a single `<56×base32>.onion`
+        # line fails closed on any such payload.
+        #
+        # It also closes the check-then-read TOCTOU: we read the file ONCE into a
+        # variable and validate that copy, so even if a symlink is raced in after
+        # the -L check, a secret's contents can never match the onion shape and
+        # are never written to the service-owned output.
+        copyOnionFile() {
+          local src=$1 dst=$2 content
+          waitForFile "$src"
+          if [[ -L $src ]]; then
+            echo "Error: refusing to read onion hostname via symlink: $src" >&2
+            exit 1
+          fi
+          content=$(cat "$src")
+          if [[ ! $content =~ ^[a-z2-7]{56}[.]onion$ ]]; then
+            echo "Error: $src does not contain exactly one v3 onion address; refusing to copy" >&2
+            exit 1
+          fi
+          install -T -m 400 <(printf '%s\n' "$content") "$dst"
+        }
+
         # Wait until tor is up
         waitForFile /var/lib/tor/state
 
+        if [[ -L ${cfg.dataDir} ]]; then
+          echo "Error: ${cfg.dataDir} is a symlink" >&2
+          exit 1
+        fi
         cd ${cfg.dataDir}
-        rm -rf ./*
+        rm -rf -- ./*
 
         ${concatMapStrings
           (user: ''
-            mkdir -p -m 0700 ${user}
-            chown ${user} ${user}
+            # Create the dir as root:root 0700 and keep it that way while writing:
+            # the target user cannot enter it, so cannot plant a symlink to race.
+            mkdir -p -m 0700 -- '${user}'
             ${concatMapStrings
               (service: ''
-                onionFile='/var/lib/tor/onion/${service}/hostname'
-                waitForFile "$onionFile"
-                cp "$onionFile" '${user}/${service}'
-                chown '${user}' '${user}/${service}'
+                copyOnionFile '/var/lib/tor/onion/${service}/hostname' '${user}/${service}'
+                chown -h '${user}' '${user}/${service}'
               '')
               cfg.access.${user}
              }
+            # Hand the fully-populated directory to the user last.
+            chown -h '${user}' '${user}'
           '')

-        ${concatMapStrings (service: ''
-          onionFile=/var/lib/tor/onion/${service}/hostname
-          waitForFile "$onionFile"
-          install -D -o ${config.systemd.services.${service}.serviceConfig.User} -m 400 "$onionFile" services/${service}
-        '') cfg.services}
+        ${optionalString (cfg.services != []) ''
+          # Root-owned, world-traversable (not writable): each service reads only
+          # its own 0400 file by exact path; no user can plant symlinks here.
+          mkdir -p -m 0755 -- services
+          ${concatMapStrings (service: ''
+            copyOnionFile '/var/lib/tor/onion/${service}/hostname' 'services/${service}'
+            chown -h '${config.systemd.services.${service}.serviceConfig.User}' 'services/${service}'
+          '') cfg.services}
+        ''}

-# RPC calls that are safe for public use
+# RPC calls that are safe for public use.
+#
+# Safety bar: bounded CPU cost per call, no P2P side effects beyond
+# transaction relay, no leaks of peer topology or wallet contents.
+#
+# Deliberately excluded (audit M-3):
+# - scantxoutset, gettxoutsetinfo, getblocktemplate: minutes-scale CPU,
+#   trivially abused for DoS by any holder of the public credentials.
+# - getblockfrompeer: lets the caller drive the node's P2P block fetching.
+# - fundrawtransaction: operates on a loaded bitcoind wallet — it selects and
+#   locks UTXOs and reveals change/wallet contents, violating the wallet-safety
+#   bar above. No local consumer (lnd, electrs, nbxplorer) uses it; lnd/nbxplorer
+#   manage their own wallets and electrs only reads blocks. Removed in the audit
+#   follow-up 2026-08-20.
+# If you need any of these, grant them via
+# `services.bitcoind.rpc.users.public.rpcwhitelist` in your own config.
+#
+# NOTE: getpeerinfo / getnodeaddresses stay in the list. This "public" user
+# is also the default RPC user that LOCAL services use, and lnd REQUIRES both
+# (its chain-backend health check + pruned-mode block fetching) — removing
+# them makes lnd fail its health check and shut itself down. They only leak
+# peer topology if you actually expose this user over a public proxy; if you
+# do that, narrow the whitelist in your own config rather than here.
