@@ -269,17 +262,47 @@ in {
           # world-readable through /proc/<pid>/cmdline
           script = nbLib.rootScript "lnd-create-macaroons" ''
             umask ug=r,o=
+            # $RUNTIME_DIRECTORY (/run/lnd) is writable by the lnd service user,
+            # so this root script must never write/chmod/chown through a path the
+            # lnd process could tamper with (else a compromised lnd escalates to
+            # arbitrary root file overwrite/chown). Staging in $RUNTIME_DIRECTORY
+            # is NOT enough — lnd owns that directory and can unlink our temp file
+            # and swap in a symlink before we write to it. Stage instead in a
+            # ROOT-ONLY directory (mode 0700, owned by root, that lnd cannot enter
+            # or replace) on the SAME tmpfs as /run/lnd, so the final move is a
+            # rename(2): atomic, and it replaces the destination without following
+            # a symlink lnd may have planted there.
+            staging=$(${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/mktemp -d "/run/lnd-macaroons.XXXXXX")
+            trap '${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/rm -rf "$staging"' EXIT
             ${lib.concatMapStrings (macaroon: ''
               echo "Create custom macaroon ${macaroon}"
-              macaroonPath="$RUNTIME_DIRECTORY/${macaroon}.macaroon"
-              adminMacaroonHex=$(${pkgs.xxd}/bin/xxd -ps -u -c 99999 '${networkDir}/admin.macaroon')
+              macaroonTmp="$staging/${macaroon}.macaroon"
+              # Read admin.macaroon AS the lnd user, never as root: it lives in
+              # lnd's own 0770 datadir, so a compromised lnd could plant a
+              # symlink there and have this root script dereference it to
+              # exfiltrate an arbitrary root-only file (backup passphrase, wg
+              # key, host keys) via the REST call below. Reading as the lnd user
+              # fails closed on such a symlink — lnd cannot read what lnd cannot
+              # read — and grants no access lnd does not already have. (The write
+              # path above is already symlink-hardened; this closes the read.)
+              adminMacaroonHex=$(${pkgs.util-linux}/bin/runuser -u ${cfg.user} -- ${pkgs.xxd}/bin/xxd -ps -u -c 99999 '${networkDir}/admin.macaroon')
+              # The response body is lnd-controlled. A macaroon is a few hundred
+              # bytes, so cap what this root script will buffer and write to the
+              # /run tmpfs at 64 KiB, and fail (jq -e) rather than write garbage
+              # when the reply carries no `.macaroon` (audit 2026-08-30, low).
               ${curl} \
                 -H @<(printf 'Grpc-Metadata-macaroon: %s\n' "$adminMacaroonHex") \
                 -X POST \
                 -d '{"permissions":[${cfg.macaroons.${macaroon}.permissions}]}' \
                 ${restUrl}/macaroon |\
-                ${pkgs.jq}/bin/jq -c '.macaroon' | ${pkgs.xxd}/bin/xxd -p -r > "$macaroonPath"
-              chown ${cfg.macaroons.${macaroon}.user}: "$macaroonPath"
+                ${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/head -c 65536 |\
+                ${pkgs.jq}/bin/jq -ce '.macaroon | strings' | ${pkgs.xxd}/bin/xxd -p -r > "$macaroonTmp"
+              chown ${cfg.macaroons.${macaroon}.user}: "$macaroonTmp"
+              # -T: the destination is a path lnd controls; without -T a
+              # symlink-to-directory planted there would make root move the
+              # file INTO that directory under the fixed basename instead of
+              # replacing the path (second opinion 2026-09-09).
+              ${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/mv -fT -- "$macaroonTmp" "$RUNTIME_DIRECTORY/${macaroon}.macaroon"
             '') (attrNames cfg.macaroons)}
           '';

    15	  attrsWith' =
    16	    placeholder: elemType:
    17	    types.attrsWith {
    18	      inherit elemType placeholder;
    19	    };
    20	
    21	  escapeArgument = lib.strings.escapeC [
    22	    "\t"
    23	    "\n"
    24	    "\r"
    25	    " "
    26	    "\\"
    27	  ];
    28	
    29	  settingsOption = {
    30	    description = ''
    31	      Declare systemd-tmpfiles rules to create, delete, and clean up volatile
    32	      and temporary files and directories.
    33	
    34	      Even though the service is called `*tmp*files` you can also create
    35	      persistent files.
    36	    '';
    37	    example = {
    38	      "10-mypackage" = {
    39	        "/var/lib/my-service/statefolder".d = {
    40	          mode = "0755";
    41	          user = "root";
    42	          group = "root";
    43	        };
    44	      };
    45	    };
    46	    default = { };
    47	    type = attrsWith' "config-name" (
    48	      attrsWith' "path" (
    49	        attrsWith' "tmpfiles-type" (
    50	          types.submodule (
    51	            { name, config, ... }:
    52	            {
    53	              options.type = mkOption {
    54	                type = types.str;
    55	                default = name;
    56	                defaultText = "‹tmpfiles-type›";
    57	                example = "d";
    58	                description = ''
    59	                  The type of operation to perform on the file.
    60	
    61	                  The type consists of a single letter and optionally one or more
    62	                  modifier characters.
    63	
    64	                  Please see the upstream documentation for the available types and
    65	                  more details:
    66	                  {manpage}`tmpfiles.d(5)`
    67	                '';
    68	              };
    69	              options.mode = mkOption {
    70	                type = types.str;
    71	                default = "-";
    72	                example = "0755";
    73	                description = ''
    74	                  The file access mode to use when creating this file or directory.
    75	                '';
    76	              };
    77	              options.user = mkOption {
    78	                type = types.str;
    79	                default = "-";
    80	                example = "root";
    81	                description = ''
    82	                  The user of the file.
    83	
    84	                  This may either be a numeric ID or a user/group name.
    85	
    86	                  If omitted or when set to `"-"`, the user and group of the user who
    87	                  invokes systemd-tmpfiles is used.
    88	                '';
    89	              };
    90	              options.group = mkOption {
    91	                type = types.str;
    92	                default = "-";
    93	                example = "root";
    94	                description = ''
    95	                  The group of the file.
    96	
    97	                  This may either be a numeric ID or a user/group name.
    98	
    99	                  If omitted or when set to `"-"`, the user and group of the user who
   100	                  invokes systemd-tmpfiles is used.
   101	                '';
   102	              };
   103	              options.age = mkOption {
   104	                type = types.str;
   105	                default = "-";
   106	                example = "10d";
   107	                description = ''
   108	                  Delete a file when it reaches a certain age.
   109	
   110	                  If a file or directory is older than the current time minus the age
   111	                  field, it is deleted.
   112	
   113	                  If set to `"-"` no automatic clean-up is done.
   114	                '';
   115	              };
   116	              options.argument = mkOption {
   117	                type = types.str;
   118	                default = "";
   119	                example = "";
   120	                description = ''
   121	                  An argument whose meaning depends on the type of operation.
   122	
   123	                  Please see the upstream documentation for the meaning of this

   125	                  {manpage}`tmpfiles.d(5)`
   126	                '';
   127	              };
   128	            }
   129	          )
   130	        )
   131	      )

   133	  };
   134	
   135	  # generates a single entry for a tmpfiles.d rule
   136	  settingsEntryToRule = path: entry: ''
   137	    '${entry.type}' '${path}' '${entry.mode}' '${entry.user}' '${entry.group}' '${entry.age}' ${escapeArgument entry.argument}
   138	  '';
   139	
   140	  # generates a list of tmpfiles.d rules from the attrs (paths) under tmpfiles.settings.<name>
   141	  pathsToRules = mapAttrsToList (
   142	    path: types: concatStrings (mapAttrsToList (_type: settingsEntryToRule path) types)
   143	  );
   144	
   145	  mkRuleFileContent = paths: concatStrings (pathsToRules paths);
   146	in
   147	{
   148	  options = {
   149	    systemd.tmpfiles.rules = mkOption {
   150	      type = types.listOf types.str;

   153	      description = ''
   154	        Rules for creation, deletion and cleaning of volatile and temporary files
   155	        automatically. See

   158	      '';

   180	      description = ''
   181	        List of packages containing {command}`systemd-tmpfiles` rules.
   182	
   183	        All files ending in .conf found in
   184	        {file}`«pkg»/lib/tmpfiles.d`
   185	        will be included.
   186	        If this folder does not exist or does not contain any files an error will be returned instead.
   187	
   188	        If a {file}`lib` output is available, rules are searched there and only there.
   189	        If there is no {file}`lib` output it will fall back to {file}`out`
   190	        and if that does not exist either, the default output will be used.
   191	      '';
   192	    };

   244	    # Allow systemd-tmpfiles to be restarted by switch-to-configuration. This
   245	    # service is not pulled into the normal boot process. It only exists for
   246	    # switch-to-configuration.
   247	    #
   248	    # This needs to be a separate unit because it does not execute
   249	    # systemd-tmpfiles with `--boot` as that is supposed to only be executed

   265	      ];
   266	      conflicts = [ "shutdown.target" ];

   268	
   269	      unitConfig.DefaultDependencies = false;
   270	
   271	      serviceConfig = {
   272	        Type = "oneshot";
   273	        RemainAfterExit = true;
   274	        ExecStart = "systemd-tmpfiles --create --remove --exclude-prefix=/dev";

   280	          "network.hosts"
   281	          "ssh.authorized_keys.root"
   282	        ];
   283	        RestrictSUIDSGID = false;
   284	      };
   285	    };

     1	# Deferred: bind lnd's REST API to the WireGuard address
     2	
     3	Status: **DEFERRED — do not implement without working through every item below.**
     4	
     5	The 2026-08-24 adversarial audit (run `2026-08-24-111031`, both models) flagged
     6	that the wireguard preset sets `services.lnd.restAddress = "0.0.0.0"`
     7	(modules/presets/wireguard.nix), exposing the macaroon-admin REST API on all
     8	interfaces and relying solely on the nixos-fw firewall (default-drop plus an
     9	accept rule for the wg subnet) for protection. Severity medium/low, advisory.
    10	The suggested fix — bind to the wg server address (`${subnet}.1`, default
    11	10.10.0.1) instead — is correct as defense-in-depth but **breaks several
    12	things that are non-obvious**, because of one load-bearing detail:
    13	
    14	> `nbLib.address` (pkgs/lib.nix) maps the bind address `0.0.0.0` to the
    15	> *connect* address `127.0.0.1`. Every internal consumer of `restAddress`
    16	> goes through it. Today they all silently talk to localhost, which the
    17	> default TLS cert covers. Bind to `10.10.0.1` and they **all** follow.
    18	
    19	## What a naive `restAddress = serverAddress` breaks
    20	
    21	1. **lnd startup becomes hard-coupled to WireGuard.** lnd fails fatally if it
    22	   cannot bind `restlisten`; the `10.10.0.1` address only exists once
    23	   `wireguard-wg-nb.service` has configured the interface. Without systemd
    24	   ordering, boot is a crash-loop race (`Restart=on-failure`/10s masks it
    25	   until it doesn't). *With* ordering, a broken wg setup (e.g. missing
    26	   `wg-server-private-key` secret) takes the whole lightning node down —
    27	   today lnd runs fine with wg broken. This coupling is inherent to the fix;
    28	   it must be a conscious acceptance, or defused with
    29	   `net.ipv4.ip_nonlocal_bind = 1` (system-wide sysctl loosening — an
    30	   auditor trade-off of its own).
    31	
    32	2. **TLS cert SANs.** The generated lnd cert only has
    33	   `DNS:localhost,IP:127.0.0.1` plus `rpcAddress` when non-local
    34	   (modules/secrets/secrets.nix `doMakeCert`; modules/lnd.nix
    35	   `certificate.extraIPs`). Nothing adds the REST address. Any validating
    36	   client connecting to `https://10.10.0.1:8080` fails TLS. The preset must
    37	   set `services.lnd.certificate.extraIPs = [ serverAddress ]`. Note
    38	   `makeCert` *does* regenerate the cert when alt-names change
    39	   (secrets.nix, `$name-cert-alt-names` comparison), so deployed nodes pick
    40	   the new SAN up on the next deploy — but anything that pinned the old cert
    41	   must tolerate rotation.
    42	
    43	3. **Macaroon creation runs in lnd's `ExecStartPost`** (modules/lnd.nix): a
    44	   root script curls `https://<nbLib.address restAddress>:<restPort>/v1` with
    45	   `--cacert`. It would move from 127.0.0.1 to 10.10.0.1 → fails without
    46	   item 2 and item 1 → **lnd.service itself fails post-start**, even though
    47	   the daemon is healthy.
    48	
    49	4. **btcpayserver's lnd-rest backend** (modules/btcpayserver.nix,
    50	   `btclightning=...server=https://<address restAddress>...`) moves to
    51	   10.10.0.1 too: needs the SAN fix and makes btcpayserver transitively
    52	   wg-dependent.
    53	
    54	5. **lndconnect onion mode** (modules/lndconnect.nix): the Tor hidden
    55	   service forwards to `nbLib.address lnd.restAddress`. Tor-based mobile
    56	   access would then depend on the wg interface being up.
    57	
    58	6. **nodeinfo** (modules/nodeinfo.nix) reports `rest_address` — cosmetic,
    59	   but scripts parsing it see a new value.
    60	
    61	## Recommended design (avoids 2–6 entirely)
    62	
    63	Do **not** touch `restAddress`. Leave it at its default `127.0.0.1` and have
    64	the preset add a *second* listener — lnd accepts repeated `restlisten`
    65	entries:
    66	
    67	```nix
    68	services.lnd.extraConfig = ''
    69	  restlisten=${serverAddress}:${toString lnd.restPort}
    70	'';
    71	```
    72	
    73	All internal consumers keep localhost (valid cert SAN, no wg coupling); the
    74	wg peer reaches `10.10.0.1:8080` exactly as before (`lndconnect-wg` already
    75	targets `serverAddress` with `--nocert`). Only item 1 — the bind-time
    76	dependency on the interface — remains, and still needs:
    77	
    78	- `systemd.services.lnd = { wants = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; after = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; }`
    79	  in the preset (runtime wg restarts are fine: a bound socket survives
    80	  interface teardown and resumes when the address returns);
    81	- the firewall accept rule stays (nixos-fw default-drops; the rule is what
    82	  admits the peer). Since 2026-09-09 it is already scoped to the interface
    83	  and the single peer (`-i wg-nb -s <peerAddress>/32`), closing the
    84	  2026-08-29 low: a spoofed-source packet on the external interface no
    85	  longer matches the rule at all, independent of rp_filter/routing.
    86	
    87	## Pre-flight checklist before shipping any variant
    88	
    89	- [ ] Decide on the lnd↔wg startup coupling (accept, or `ip_nonlocal_bind`).
    90	- [ ] `test/wireguard-lndconnect.nix`: assert lnd comes up cleanly from a
    91	      cold boot (ordering race), REST reachable over wg at
    92	      `serverAddress:restPort`, REST **not** bound on the external
    93	      interface (the actual point of the fix), macaroon `ExecStartPost`
    94	      succeeded, and — if btcpayserver is in the scenario — its lnd backend
    95	      connects.
    96	- [ ] Full local VM suite green (`./test/run-tests.sh`).
    97	- [ ] Verify on a staging deploy that cert regeneration (if `restAddress`
    98	      was changed rather than the extraConfig variant) does not strand
    99	      existing clients.
   100	- [ ] Re-run the adversarial LLM audit (audits branch) — per policy every
   101	      fix re-runs the whole review; the review should specifically re-check
   102	      items 1–6.

     3	# Workflow for releasing a new nix-bitcoin version with incompatible changes:
     4	# Let V be the version of the upcoming, incompatible release.
     5	# 1. Add change descriptions with `version = V` at the end of the `changes` list below.
     6	# 2. Set `nix-bitcoin.configVersion = V` in ../examples/configuration.nix.
     7	
     8	with lib;
     9	let
    10	  options = {
    11	    nix-bitcoin.configVersion = mkOption {
    12	      type = with types; nullOr str;
    13	      default = null;
    14	      example = "0.0.92";
    15	      description = ''
    16	        The nix-bitcoin release version that your config is compatible with.
    17	
    18	        When upgrading to a backwards-incompatible release, nix-bitcoin will throw an
    19	        error during evaluation and provide instructions for migrating your config to
    20	        the new release.
    21	
    22	        Once set, you only need to update this option when explicitly told to in an
    23	        error message during evaluation.
    24	      '';
    25	    };
    26	  };
    27	
    28	  # Sorted by increasing version numbers.
    29	  # Historical upstream migration notices (0.0.26 - 0.0.139) were dropped in
    30	  # the fork trim: they all concerned services this fork no longer ships, and
    31	  # several referenced their (now removed) config options.
    32	  changes = [
    33	    {
    34	      version = "0.0.140";
    35	      condition = false; # informational marker only, never fires
    36	      message = ''
    37	        This fork was trimmed to the services its maintainers deploy:
    38	        bitcoind, lnd, electrs, btcpayserver/nbxplorer, lndconnect and the
    39	        supporting infrastructure. clightning (and its plugins/REST servers),
    40	        joinmarket, rtl, mempool, fulcrum, liquid, lightning-loop,
    41	        lightning-pool, charge-lnd and hardware-wallets were removed.
    42	        The last revision with the full upstream set is tag v0.0.139.
    43	      '';
    44	    }
    45	  ];
    46	
    47	  version = config.nix-bitcoin.configVersion;
    48	
    49	  incompatibleChanges = optionals
    50	    (version != null && versionOlder lastChange)
    51	    (builtins.filter (change: versionOlder change && (change.condition or true)) changes);
    52	
    53	  errorMsg = ''
    54	
    55	    This version of nix-bitcoin contains the following changes
    56	    that are incompatible with your config (version ${version}):
    57	
    58	    ${concatMapStringsSep "\n" (change: ''
    59	      - ${change.message}(This change was introduced in version ${change.version})
    60	    '') incompatibleChanges}
    61	    After addressing the above changes, set nix-bitcoin.configVersion = "${lastChange.version}";
    62	    in your nix-bitcoin configuration.
    63	  '';
    64	
    65	  versionOlder = change: (builtins.compareVersions change.version version) > 0;
    66	  lastChange = builtins.elemAt changes (builtins.length changes - 1);
    67	in
    68	{
    69	  imports = [
    70	    ./obsolete-options.nix
    71	  ];
    72	
    73	  inherit options;
    74	
    75	  config = {
    76	    # Force evaluation. An actual option value is never assigned
    77	    system = optionalAttrs (builtins.length incompatibleChanges > 0) (builtins.throw errorMsg);
    78	  };
    79	}

I found a possible switch-time regression: the new tmpfiles rule sets the secrets directory to `0700`, while setup-secrets later sets it to `0751`. I’m checking whether a routine rebuild can reapply `0700` without rerunning setup-secrets. Normal flake evaluation is blocked by the read-only environment, but I found the exact pinned nixpkgs sources locally and can evaluate selected options without accessing the daemon.

     1	#!/usr/bin/env nix-shell
     2	#!nix-shell -i bash -p bash coreutils curl jq gnupg gnugrep
     3	set -euo pipefail
     4	
     5	scriptDir=$(cd "${BASH_SOURCE[0]%/*}" && pwd)
     6	
     7	trap 'echo "Error at ${BASH_SOURCE[0]}:$LINENO"' ERR
     8	
     9	repo=fort-nix/nix-bitcoin
    10	
    11	# This legacy helper fetches an UPSTREAM (fort-nix) signed release tarball. This
    12	# repository is a maintained FORK; upstream's release artifacts do NOT contain
    13	# this fork's fixes, so fetching them here would silently ship un-fixed code.
    14	# Consume this fork via its flake pin instead:
    15	#   inputs.nix-bitcoin.url = "github:ostermayer/nix-bitcoin/release";
    16	# Refuse to run unless explicitly overridden.
    17	if [[ ${NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM:-} != 1 ]]; then
    18	    echo "This is a fork of nix-bitcoin; pin the flake, don't fetch releases:" >&2
    19	    echo "  inputs.nix-bitcoin.url = \"github:ostermayer/nix-bitcoin/release\";" >&2
    20	    echo "This helper fetches UPSTREAM ($repo) releases, WITHOUT the fork's fixes." >&2
    21	    echo "Set NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM=1 only if you truly want upstream." >&2
    22	    exit 1
    23	fi
    24	
    25	if [[ ! -v version ]]; then
    26	    version=$(curl -fsS "https://api.github.com/repos/$repo/releases/latest" | jq -r '.tag_name' | tail -c +2)
    27	fi
    28	
    29	TMPDIR=$(mktemp -d)
    30	trap 'rm -rf $TMPDIR' EXIT
    31	
    32	export GNUPGHOME=$TMPDIR/gpg-home
    33	mkdir -m 700 "$GNUPGHOME"
    34	
    35	# Import key
    36	gpg --import "$scriptDir/key-jonasnick.bin" &> /dev/null
    37	# Check that exactly one key was imported
    38	(($(gpg --list-keys --with-colons | grep -c pub) == 1))
    39	# Verify key fingerprint
    40	gpg --list-keys "36C7 1A37 C9D9 88BD E825 08D9 B1A7 0E4F 8DCD 0366" > /dev/null
    41	
    42	# Fetch nar-hash of release
    43	cd "$TMPDIR"
    44	baseUrl=https://github.com/$repo/releases/download/v$version
    45	curl -fsS -L -O "$baseUrl/nar-hash.txt"
    46	curl -fsS -L -O "$baseUrl/nar-hash.txt.asc"
    47	
    48	# Verify signature for nar-hash
    49	gpg --verify nar-hash.txt.asc nar-hash.txt &> /dev/null || {
    50	    >&2 echo "Error: Signature verification failed. Please open an issue in the project repository."
    51	    exit 1
    52	}
    53	
    54	>&2 echo "Fetched and verified release $version"
    55	
    56	cat <<EOF
    57	builtins.fetchTarball {
    58	  url = "https://github.com/$repo/archive/v$version.tar.gz";
    59	  sha256 = "$(cat nar-hash.txt)";
    60	}
    61	EOF

    14	  name = "nix-bitcoin-environment";
    15	
    16	  helpMessage = ''
    17	    nix-bitcoin path: ${toString ../.}
    18	
    19	    Available commands
    20	    ==================
    21	    deploy
    22	      Run krops-deploy and eval-config in parallel.
    23	      This ensures that eval failures appear quickly when deploying.
    24	      In this case, deployment is stopped.
    25	
    26	    krops-deploy
    27	      Deploy your node via krops
    28	
    29	    eval-config
    30	      Evaluate your node system configuration
    31	
    32	    build-config
    33	       Build your node system on your local machine
    34	
    35	    generate-secrets
    36	      Create secrets required by your node configuration.
    37	      Secrets are written to ./secrets/
    38	      This function is automatically called by krops-deploy.
    39	
    40	    update-nix-bitcoin
    41	      Fetch and use the latest version of nix-bitcoin
    42	  '';

    44	  shellHook = ''
    45	    export NIX_PATH="nixpkgs=${nixpkgs}:nix-bitcoin=${toString ../.}:."
    46	    ${setPath}
    47	    export NIX_BITCOIN_EXAMPLES_DIR="${cfgDir}"
    48	    export nixpkgsUnstable="${pinned.nixpkgs-unstable}"
    49

    51	    # 1. stdout is a TTY, i.e. we're not piping the output
    52	    # 2. the shell is interactive
    53	    if [[ -t 1 && $- == *i* ]]; then isInteractive=1; else isInteractive=; fi
    54

    59	    h() { help; }

    61	    fetch-release() {
    62	      ${toString ./fetch-release}
    63	    }
    64	
    65	    update-nix-bitcoin() {(
    66	      # Fork-native pin: resolve the fork's latest CalVer release tag to the
    67	      # commit it points at, prefetch that commit's tarball and rewrite
    68	      # nix-bitcoin-release.nix. Never fetches upstream fort-nix releases
    69	      # (they lack this fork's fixes). Tags are unsigned CI pointers — the
    70	      # commit is what you review; see SECURITY.md.
    71	      set -euo pipefail
    72	      releaseFile="${cfgDir}/nix-bitcoin-release.nix"
    73	      repo=https://github.com/ostermayer/nix-bitcoin
    74	      tag=$(git ls-remote --tags --refs "$repo" 'refs/tags/20*' | sed 's|.*refs/tags/||' | sort -V | tail -n1)
    75	      [[ $tag ]] || { echo "No release tag found on $repo" >&2; exit 1; }
    76	      commit=$(git ls-remote "$repo" "refs/tags/$tag^{}" | cut -f1)
    77	      [[ $commit ]] || commit=$(git ls-remote "$repo" "refs/tags/$tag" | cut -f1)
    78	      url="$repo/archive/$commit.tar.gz"
    79	      hash=$(nix-prefetch-url --unpack "$url" 2>/dev/null)
    80	      new=$(printf 'builtins.fetchTarball {\n  # fork release %s\n  url = "%s";\n  sha256 = "%s";\n}\n' "$tag" "$url" "$hash")
    81	      current=$(cat "$releaseFile" 2>/dev/null || true)
    82	      if [[ $new == "$current" ]]; then
    83	        echo "nix-bitcoin-release.nix already pins $tag ($commit)"
    84	      else
    85	        printf '%s' "$new" > "$releaseFile"
    86	        echo "Pinned $tag ($commit) in nix-bitcoin-release.nix."
    87	        echo "Review that commit first — the next nix-shell evaluates the new tree's shellHook:"
    88	        echo "  https://github.com/ostermayer/nix-bitcoin/compare/$(echo "$current" | sed -n 's|.*/archive/\([0-9a-f]*\)\.tar\.gz.*|\1|p')...$commit"
    89	        echo "then re-enter the deployment shell with: exit; nix-shell"
    90	        # Deliberately no automatic `exec nix-shell`: that would run the newly
    91	        # pinned revision's shell code on this machine before the review the
    92	        # line above asks for (second opinion 2026-09-09).
    93	      fi
    94	    )}
    95	
    96	    generate-secrets() {(
    97	      set -euo pipefail
    98	      config="${cfgDir}/krops/krops-configuration.nix"
    99	      if [[ ! -e $config ]]; then
   100	        config="${cfgDir}/configuration.nix"
   101	      fi
   102	      genSecrets=$(nix-build --no-out-link -I nixos-config="$config" \
   103	                   '<nixpkgs/nixos>' -A config.nix-bitcoin.generateSecretsScript)
   104	      mkdir -p "${cfgDir}/secrets"
   105	      (cd "${cfgDir}/secrets"; $genSecrets)
   106	    )}
   107	
   108	    deploy() {(
   109	      set -euo pipefail
   110	      krops-deploy &
   111	      kropsPid=$!
   112	      if eval-config; then
   113	        wait $kropsPid
   114	      else
   115	        # Kill all subprocesses
   116	        kill $(pidClosure $kropsPid)
   117	        return 1
   118	      fi
   119	    )}
   120	
   121	    krops-deploy() {(
   122	      set -euo pipefail
   123	      generate-secrets
   124	      # Ensure strict permissions on secrets/ directory before rsyncing it to
   125	      # the target machine
   126	      chmod 700 "${cfgDir}/secrets"
   127	      $(nix-build --no-out-link "${cfgDir}/krops/deploy.nix")
   128	    )}
   129	
   130	    eval-config() {(
   131	      set -euo pipefail
   132	      system=$(getNodeSystem)
   133	      NIXOS_CONFIG="${cfgDir}/krops/krops-configuration.nix" \
   134	        nix-instantiate --eval ${nixpkgs}/nixos $system -A system.outPath | tr -d '"'
   135	      echo
   136	    )}
   137	
   138	    build-config() {(
   139	      set -euo pipefail
   140	      system=$(getNodeSystem)
   141	      NIXOS_CONFIG="${cfgDir}/krops/krops-configuration.nix" \
   142	        nix-build --no-out-link ${nixpkgs}/nixos $system -A system
   143	    )}
   144	
   145	    getNodeSystem() {
   146	      if [[ -e '${cfgDir}/krops/system' ]]; then
   147	        echo -n "--argstr system "; cat '${cfgDir}/krops/system'
   148	      elif [[ $OSTYPE == darwin* ]]; then
   149	        # On macOS, `builtins.currentSystem` (`*-darwin`) can never equal
   150	        # the node system (`*-linux`), so we can always provide a helpful error message:
   151	        >&2 echo "Error, node system not set. See here how to fix this:"
   152	        >&2 echo "https://github.com/fort-nix/nix-bitcoin/blob/master/docs/install.md#optional-specify-the-system-of-your-node"
   153	        return 1
   154	      fi
   155	    };
   156	
   157	    pidClosure() {
   158	      echo "$1"
   159	      for pid in $(ps -o pid= --ppid "$1"); do
   160	        pidClosure "$pid"
   161	      done
   162	    }
   163	
   164	    if [[ $isInteractive ]]; then
   165	      ${pkgs.figlet}/bin/figlet "nix-bitcoin"
   166	      echo 'Enter "h" or "help" for documentation.'
   167	    fi
   168	
   169	    # Don't run this hook when another nix-shell is run inside this shell
   170	    unset shellHook
   171	
   172	    ${extraShellInitCmds pkgs}
   173	  '';
   174	}
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	
     3	# Usage:
     4	# source "path/to/run-in-nix-env" "pkg1 pkg2 ..." "$@"
     5	#
     6	# Runs the calling script with pkgs1, pkg2, ... in PATH.
     7	# The packages are sourced from the nixpkgs input of the nix-bitcoin root flake.
     8	
     9	if [[ ${_NIX_BITCOIN_ENV_PKGS:-} != "$1" ]]; then
    10	    export _NIX_BITCOIN_ENV_PKGS=$1
    11	    shift
    12	
    13	    _shell_pkgs=()
    14	    for _pkg in $_NIX_BITCOIN_ENV_PKGS; do
    15	      _shell_pkgs+=("nixpkgs#$_pkg")
    16	    done
    17	    # BASH_SOURCE[0] is this file
    18	    # BASH_SOURCE[-1] is the root src file
    19	    exec nix shell --inputs-from "${BASH_SOURCE[0]%/*}/.." "${_shell_pkgs[@]}" -c "${BASH_SOURCE[-1]}" "$@"
    20	fi
     1	# Start an interactive bash session in the current bash environment.
     2	# This is helpful for debugging bash scripts like pkg update scripts,
     3	# by adding `source <path-to>/start-bash-session.sh` at the location to
     4	# be inspected.
     5	
     6	
     7	# BASH_ENVIRONMENT contains definitions of read-only variables like 'BASHOPTS' that
     8	# cause warnings on evaluation. Suppress these warnings while sourcing.
     9	#
    10	# shellcheck disable=SC2016
    11	BASH_ENVIRONMENT=<(declare -p; declare -pf) \
    12	  bash --rcfile <(echo 'source $BASH_ENVIRONMENT 2>/dev/null')
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	
     3	set -euo pipefail
     4	
     5	# The file that defines the derivation that should be updated
     6	file=$1
     7	# The name of the output of this flake that should be updated
     8	flakeOutput=$2
     9	# A pattern in a line preceding the hash that should be updated
    10	patternPrecedingHash=$3
    11	
    12	sed -i "/$patternPrecedingHash/,/hash/ s|hash = .*|hash = \"\";|" "$file"
    13	# Display stderr and capture it. stdbuf is required to disable output buffering.
    14	stderr=$(
    15	    nix build --no-link -L ".#$flakeOutput" |&
    16	      stdbuf -oL grep -v '\berror:.*failed to build$' |
    17	      tee /dev/stderr || :
    18	)
    19	hash=$(echo "$stderr" | sed -nE 's/.*?\bgot: *?(sha256-.*)/\1/p')
    20	if [[ ! $hash ]]; then
    21	    echo
    22	    echo "Error: No hash in build output."
    23	    exit 1
    24	fi
    25	sed -i "/$patternPrecedingHash/,/hash/ s|hash = .*|hash = \"$hash\";|" "$file"
    26	echo "(Note: The above hash mismatch message is not an error. It is part of the fetching process.)"
     1	{ prevVersions ? null }:
     2	let
     3	  flake = builtins.getFlake (toString ../.);
     4	  inherit (flake.inputs.nixpkgs) lib;
     5	in rec {
     6	  # Convert the list of pinned pkgs to an attrset with form
     7	  # {
     8	  #   stable = { bitcoind = "0.21.1"; ... };
     9	  #   unstable = { btcpayserver = "1.2.1"; ... };
    10	  # }
    11	  # A pinned pkg is added to `stable` if its stable version is newer or
    12	  # identical to the unstable version.
    13	  versions = let
    14	    pinned = flake.legacyPackages.x86_64-linux.pinned;
    15	    pinnedPkgs = lib.filterAttrs (n: v: lib.isDerivation v) pinned;
    16	    stable = pinned.pkgs;
    17	    unstable = pinned.pkgsUnstable;
    18	    isStable = builtins.partition (pkgName:
    19	      !(unstable ? "${pkgName}") ||
    20	      ((stable ? "${pkgName}")
    21	       && (builtins.compareVersions stable.${pkgName}.version unstable.${pkgName}.version >= 0))
    22	    ) (builtins.attrNames pinnedPkgs);
    23	  in {
    24	    stable   = lib.genAttrs isStable.right (pkgName: stable.${pkgName}.version);
    25	    unstable = lib.genAttrs isStable.wrong (pkgName: unstable.${pkgName}.version);
    26	  };
    27	
    28	  showUpdates = let
    29	    prev = builtins.fromJSON prevVersions;
    30	    prevPkgs = prev.stable // prev.unstable;
    31	    mapFilter = f: xs: lib.remove null (map f xs);
    32	
    33	    mkChanges = title: pkgs:
    34	      let
    35	        lines = mapFilter (pkgName:
    36	          let
    37	            version = pkgs.${pkgName};
    38	            prevVersion = prevPkgs.${pkgName};
    39	          in
    40	            if version != prevVersion then
    41	              "${pkgName}: ${prevVersion} -> ${version}"
    42	            else
    43	              null
    44	        ) (builtins.attrNames pkgs);
    45	      in
    46	        if lines == [] then
    47	             null
    48	        else
    49	          builtins.concatStringsSep "\n" ([title] ++ lines);
    50	
    51	    changes = [
    52	      (mkChanges "Pkg updates in nixpkgs stable:" versions.stable)
    53	      (mkChanges "Pkg updates in nixpkgs unstable:" versions.unstable)
    54	    ];
    55	
    56	    allChanges = builtins.concatStringsSep "\n\n" (lib.remove null changes);
    57	  in
    58	    if allChanges == "" then
    59	      "No pkg version updates."
    60	    else
    61	      allChanges;
    62	
    63	  pinnedFile = let
    64	    toLines = pkgs: builtins.concatStringsSep "\n    " (builtins.attrNames pkgs);
    65	  in ''
    66	    # This file is generated by ../helper/update-flake.nix
    67	    pkgs: pkgsUnstable:
    68	    {
    69	      inherit (pkgs)
    70	        ${toLines versions.stable};
    71	
    72	      inherit (pkgsUnstable)
    73	        ${toLines versions.unstable};
    74	
    75	      inherit pkgs pkgsUnstable;
    76	    }
    77	  '';
    78	}
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	set -euo pipefail
     3	
     4	# This script does the following:
     5	# - When called without arguments, update all flake inputs, including nixpkgs.
     6	# - When called with a version argument, set input `nixpkgs` in `flake.nix` to the
     7	#   specified version and only update this input.
     8	# - Print version updates of pinned pkgs like so:
     9	#     Pkg updates in nixpkgs unstable:
    10	#     bitcoin: 0.20.0 -> 0.21.1
    11	#     btcpayserver: 1.1.0 -> 1.1.2
    12	# - Write ../pkgs/pinned.nix:
    13	#     Packages for which the stable und unstable versions are identical are
    14	#     pinned to stable.
    15	#     All other pkgs are pinned to unstable.
    16	
    17	forceRun=
    18	nixosVersion=
    19	for arg in "$@"; do
    20	    case $arg in
    21	        -f)
    22	            forceRun=1
    23	            ;;
    24	        *)
    25	            nixosVersion=$arg
    26	            ;;
    27	    esac
    28	done
    29	
    30	# cd to script dir
    31	cd "${BASH_SOURCE[0]%/*}"
    32	
    33	if [[ $(nix flake 2>&1) != *"requires a sub-command"* ]]; then
    34	    echo "Error. This script requires nix flake support."
    35	    echo "https://nixos.wiki/wiki/Flakes#Installing_flakes"
    36	    exit 1
    37	fi
    38	
    39	if [[ ! $forceRun ]] && ! git diff --quiet ../flake.{nix,lock}; then
    40	    echo "error: flake.nix/flake.lock have changes. Run with option -f to ignore."
    41	    exit 1
    42	fi
    43	
    44	if [[ ! $nixosVersion ]]; then
    45	    echo "Updating flake 'nixos-search'"
    46	    nix flake update --flake ../test/nixos-search
    47	    echo
    48	fi
    49	
    50	versions=$(nix eval --json -f update-flake.nix versions)
    51	
    52	## Uncomment the following to generate a version change message for testing
    53	# versions=$(echo "$versions" | sed 's|1|0|g')
    54	
    55	setVersion() {
    56	    sed -Ei "s|($1)[0-9.]+|\1$nixosVersion|" "$2"
    57	}
    58	
    59	echo "Updating main flake"
    60	if [[ $nixosVersion ]]; then
    61	    setVersion 'nixpkgs.url = .*?nixos-' ../flake.nix
    62	    setVersion 'system.stateVersion = "' ../examples/configuration.nix
    63	    setVersion 'nix-bitcoin.url = .*?/nixos-' ../examples/flakes/flake.nix
    64	    setVersion 'nix-bitcoin.url = .*?/nixos-' ../examples/container/flake.nix
    65	    setVersion 'update-flake.sh ' ../dev/README.md
    66	    nix flake update nixpkgs --flake ..
    67	else
    68	    nix flake update --flake ..
    69	fi
    70	
    71	echo
    72	nix eval --raw -f update-flake.nix --argstr prevVersions "$versions" showUpdates; echo
    73	
    74	pinned=../pkgs/pinned.nix
    75	pinnedSrc=$(nix eval --raw -f update-flake.nix --argstr prevVersions "$versions" pinnedFile)
    76	if [[ $pinnedSrc != $(cat "$pinned") ]]; then
    77	    echo "$pinnedSrc" > "$pinned"
    78	    echo
    79	    echo "Updated pinned.nix"
    80	fi
{ pkgs ? import <nixpkgs> {} }:

    16	      nixpkgs.pkgs = pkgs;
    17	
    18	      nix-bitcoin.generateSecrets = true;
    19	      nix-bitcoin.operator.enable = true;
    20	
    21	      services.lnd = {
    22	        enable = true;
    23	        lndconnect.enable = true;
    24	        port = 9736;
    25	      };
    26	    };

    28	    client = {
    29	      nixpkgs.pkgs = pkgs;
    30	
    31	      environment.systemPackages = with pkgs; [
    32	        wireguard-tools
    33	      ];
    34	    };

    37	  testScript =  ''
    38	    import base64
    39	    import urllib.parse as Url
    40	    from types import SimpleNamespace
    41	
    42	    def parse_lndconnect_url(url):
    43	        u = Url.urlparse(url)
    44	        data = {'host': u.hostname, 'port': u.port}
    45	        queries = Url.parse_qs(u.query)
    46	        macaroon = queries['macaroon'][0]
    47	        data['macaroon_hex'] = base64.urlsafe_b64decode(macaroon + '===').hex().upper()
    48	        return SimpleNamespace(**data)
    49	
    50	    client.start()
    51	    server.connect()
    52	
    53	    if not "is_interactive" in vars():
    54	
    55	      with subtest("connect client to server via WireGuard"):
    56	          server.wait_for_unit("wireguard-wg-nb-peer-peer0.service")
    57	
    58	          # Get WireGuard config from server and save it to `/tmp/wireguard.conf` on the client
    59	          wg_config = server.succeed("runuser -u operator -- nix-bitcoin-wg-connect server --text")
    60	          # Encode to base64
    61	          b64 = base64.b64encode(wg_config.encode('utf-8')).decode()
    62	          client.succeed(f"install -m 400 <(echo -n {b64} | base64 -d) /tmp/wireguard.conf")
    63	
    64	          # Connect to server via WireGuard
    65	          client.succeed("wg-quick up /tmp/wireguard.conf")
    66	
    67	          # Ping server from client
    68	          print(client.succeed("ping -c 1 -W 0.5 10.10.0.1"))
    69	
    70	      with subtest("lndconnect-wg"):
    71	          server.wait_for_unit("lnd.service")
    72	          lndconnect_url = server.succeed("runuser -u operator -- lndconnect-wg --url")
    73	          api = parse_lndconnect_url(lndconnect_url)
    74	          # Make lnd REST API call
    75	          client.succeed(
    76	              f"curl -fsS --max-time 3 --insecure --header 'Grpc-Metadata-macaroon: {api.macaroon_hex}' "
    77	              f"-X GET https://{api.host}:{api.port}/v1/getinfo"
    78	          )
    79	  '';
    80	}
     1	from collections import OrderedDict
     2	import json
     3	import re
     4	
     5	def succeed(*cmds):
     6	    """Returns the concatenated output of all cmds"""
     7	    return machine.succeed(*cmds)
     8	
     9	def assert_matches(cmd, regexp):
    10	    assert_str_matches(succeed(cmd), regexp)
    11	
    12	def assert_str_matches(str, regexp):
    13	    if not re.search(regexp, str):
    14	        raise Exception(f"Pattern '{regexp}' not found in '{str}'")
    15	
    16	def assert_full_match(cmd, regexp):
    17	    out = succeed(cmd)
    18	    if not re.fullmatch(regexp, out):
    19	        raise Exception(f"Pattern '{regexp}' doesn't match '{out}'")
    20	
    21	def log_has_string(unit, str):
    22	    return f"journalctl -b --output=cat -u {unit} --grep='{str}'"
    23	
    24	def assert_no_failure(unit):
    25	    """Unit should not have failed since the system is running"""
    26	    machine.fail(log_has_string(unit, "Failed with result"))
    27	
    28	def assert_running(unit):
    29	    with machine.nested(f"waiting for unit: {unit}"):
    30	        machine.wait_for_unit(unit)
    31	    assert_no_failure(unit)
    32	
    33	def wait_for_open_port(address, port):
    34	    def is_port_open(_):
    35	        status, _ = machine.execute(f"nc -z {address} {port}")
    36	        return status == 0
    37	
    38	    with machine.nested(f"Waiting for TCP port {address}:{port}"):
    39	        retry(is_port_open)
    40	
    41	
    42	### Test runner
    43	
    44	tests = OrderedDict()
    45	
    46	def test(name):
    47	    def x(fn):
    48	        tests[name] = fn
    49	    return x
    50	
    51	# `run_tests` is already defined by the NixOS test driver
    52	def nb_run_tests():
    53	    enabled = enabled_tests.copy()
    54	    to_run = []
    55	    for test in tests:
    56	        if test in enabled:
    57	            enabled.remove(test)
    58	            to_run.append(test)
    59	    if enabled:
    60	        raise RuntimeError(f"The following tests are enabled but not defined: {enabled}")
    61	    machine.connect()  # Visually separate boot output from the test output
    62	    for test in to_run:
    63	        with machine.nested(f"test: {test}"):
    64	            tests[test]()
    65	
    66	def run_test(test):
    67	    tests[test]()
    68

    73	@test("security")
    74	def _():
    75	    assert_running("setup-secrets")
    76	    # Unused secrets should be inaccessible
    77	    succeed('[[ $(stat -c "%U:%G %a" /secrets/dummy) = "root:root 440" ]]')
    78	
    79	    if "secure-node" in enabled_tests:
    80	        machine.wait_for_unit("bitcoind")
    81	        # `systemctl status` run by unprivileged users shouldn't leak cgroup info
    82	        assert_matches(
    83	            "runuser -u electrs -- systemctl status bitcoind 2>&1 >/dev/null",
    84	            "Failed to dump process list for 'bitcoind.service', ignoring: Access denied",
    85	        )
    86	        # The 'operator' with group 'proc' has full access
    87	        assert_full_match("runuser -u operator -- systemctl status bitcoind 2>&1 >/dev/null", "")

   134	@test("lnd-macaroon-read-deprivileged")
   135	def _():
   136	    # Regression test for the 2026-08-30 audit finding (modules/lnd.nix): the
   137	    # root ExecStartPost that mints custom macaroons must read admin.macaroon AS
   138	    # THE LND USER, never as root. admin.macaroon sits in lnd's own 0770 datadir,
   139	    # so a compromised lnd can replace it with a symlink to a root-only file; if
   140	    # root dereferences it, the bytes are exfiltrated to lnd via the REST call.
   141	    # Reading as the lnd user fails closed on such a symlink.
   142	    assert_running("lnd")
   143	
   144	    # (a) Pin the deployed unit: the macaroon read must go through the run-as-lnd
   145	    #     wrapper. Reverting the fix to a bare root `xxd ... admin.macaroon`
   146	    #     makes this grep fail.
   147	    script = succeed(
   148	        "systemctl show lnd -p ExecStartPost --value | "
   149	        "grep -oE '/nix/store/[a-z0-9]+-lnd-create-macaroons' | head -1"
   150	    ).strip()
   151	    assert script, "lnd-create-macaroons ExecStartPost script not found"
   152	    assert_matches(
   153	        f"cat {script}",
   154	        r"runuser -u lnd [^\n]*xxd[^\n]*admin\.macaroon",
   155	    )
   156	
   157	    # (b) Prove the runtime asymmetry the fix relies on, in lnd's real datadir,
   158	    #     as the real lnd user, without disrupting the service. The canary sits
   159	    #     in a traversable dir (/run) but is root-only readable, mirroring the
   160	    #     real targets (backup passphrase, wg key, host keys).
   161	    succeed(
   162	        "printf ROOT_ONLY_CANARY > /run/macaroon-canary",
   163	        "chown root:root /run/macaroon-canary",
   164	        "chmod 400 /run/macaroon-canary",
   165	    )
   166	    link = "/var/lib/lnd/attack-test-link"
   167	    # The attacker is the lnd user, which owns its 0770 datadir.
   168	    succeed(f"runuser -u lnd -- ln -sf /run/macaroon-canary {link}")
   169	    # Root WOULD read the canary through the symlink (the exploit precondition).
   170	    assert_matches(f"cat {link}", "ROOT_ONLY_CANARY")
   171	    # The lnd user must NOT be able to — so the fix's read fails closed.
   172	    machine.fail(f"runuser -u lnd -- cat {link}")
   173	    succeed(f"rm -f {link} /run/macaroon-canary")
   174	
   175	@test("lndconnect-onion-lnd")
   176	def _():
   177	    assert_running("lnd")
   178	    assert_matches("runuser -u operator -- lndconnect --url", ".onion")

   180	@test("btcpayserver")
   181	def _():
   182	    assert_running("nbxplorer")
   183	    machine.wait_until_succeeds(log_has_string("nbxplorer", "BTC: RPC connection successful"))
   184	    wait_for_open_port(ip("nbxplorer"), 24444)
   185	    # Regression guard: NBXplorer's .NET host takes its content root from the
   186	    # working directory and (by default) watches it recursively. With no
   187	    # WorkingDirectory that was `/`, i.e. an inotify watch per directory of the
   188	    # whole root filesystem (the Nix store included), which exhausted the
   189	    # per-user watch limit on a production node in days. The content root must
   190	    # be the immutable app dir (a writable one would honor a planted
   191	    # appsettings.json), and with reload-on-change off nothing is watched.
   192	    nbx_pid = succeed("systemctl show -p MainPID --value nbxplorer").strip()
   193	    assert_matches(f"readlink /proc/{nbx_pid}/cwd", r"^/nix/store/[^/]+-nbxplorer-[^/]+/lib/nbxplorer$")
   194	    nbx_watches = int(succeed(f"cat /proc/{nbx_pid}/fdinfo/* 2>/dev/null | grep -c '^inotify wd:' || true").strip() or 0)
   195	    assert nbx_watches < 100, f"nbxplorer holds {nbx_watches} inotify watches"
   196	
   197	    assert_running("btcpayserver")
   198	    machine.wait_until_succeeds(log_has_string("btcpayserver", "Now listening on"))
   199	    wait_for_open_port(ip("btcpayserver"), 23000)
   200	    # test lnd custom macaroon
   201	    assert_matches(
   202	        "runuser -u btcpayserver -- curl -fsS --cacert /secrets/lnd-cert "
   203	        '--header "Grpc-Metadata-macaroon: $(xxd -ps -u -c 1000 /run/lnd/btcpayserver.macaroon)" '
   204	        f"-X GET https://{ip('lnd')}:8080/v1/getinfo | jq",
   205	        '"version"',
   206	    )
   207	    # Test web server response
   208	    assert_matches(f"curl -fsS -L {ip('btcpayserver')}:23000", "Welcome to BTCPay Server")

   223	# Run this test before the following tests that shut down services
   224	# (and their corresponding network namespaces).
   225	@test("netns-isolation")
   226	def _():
   227	    def get_ips(services):
   228	        enabled = enabled_tests.intersection(services)
   229	        return " ".join(ip(service) for service in enabled)
   230	
   231	    def assert_reachable(src, dests):
   232	        dest_ips = get_ips(dests)
   233	        if src in enabled_tests and dest_ips:
   234	            machine.succeed(f"ip netns exec nb-{src} fping -c1 -t100 {dest_ips}")
   235	
   236	    def assert_unreachable(src, dests):
   237	        dest_ips = get_ips(dests)
   238	        if src in enabled_tests and dest_ips:
   239	            machine.fail(
   240	                # This fails when no host is reachable within 100 ms
   241	                f"ip netns exec nb-{src} fping -c1 -t100 --reachable=1 {dest_ips}"
   242	            )
   243	
   244	    # These reachability tests are non-exhaustive
   245	    assert_reachable("bitcoind", ["clightning", "lnd", "liquidd"])
   246	    assert_unreachable("bitcoind", ["btcpayserver", "rtl", "lightning-loop"])
   247	    assert_unreachable("btcpayserver", ["bitcoind", "lightning-loop"])
   248	
   249	    # netns addresses can not be bound to in the main netns.
   250	    # This prevents processes in the main netns from impersonating nix-bitcoin services.
   251	    assert_matches(
   252	        f"nc -l {ip('bitcoind')} 1080 2>&1 || true", "nc: Cannot assign requested address"
   253	    )
   254	
   255	    # netns-exec should fail for unauthorized namespaces
   256	    assert_matches(
   257	        "runuser -u operator -- netns-exec nb-clightning ip a 2>&1 || true",
   258	        "nb-clightning is not an allowed netns",
   259	    )
   260	
   261	    # netns-exec should only be executable by the operator user.
   262	    # User `unauthorized` is a member of group `users`, like all normal users.
   263	    # Netns `nb-clightning` is rejected by netns-exec before it accesses the
   264	    # netns, so the error below can only originate from the exec permissions.
   265	    assert_matches(
   266	        "runuser -u unauthorized -- netns-exec nb-clightning ip a 2>&1 || true",
   267	        "Permission denied",
   268	    )
   269	
   270	    # netns-exec should drop capabilities. nb-bitcoind is always present in a
   271	    # netns scenario; netns-exec requires an absolute command path.
   272	    assert_matches(
   273	        "runuser -u operator -- netns-exec nb-bitcoind $(command -v capsh) --print | grep Current",
   274	        re.compile("^Current: =$", re.MULTILINE),
   275	    )
   276

name: nix-bitcoin tests

on:
  push:
    # Code branches only. The `audits` branch carries generated LLM-audit
    # transcripts, not code — running the VM suite on it would be pure waste.
    # `auto/**` is the weekly fork-autotest pin candidate: its CANDIDATE READY
    # email promises GitHub CI revalidation, which this trigger provides.
    branches: [master, release, "auto/**"]
  pull_request:
  workflow_dispatch:

env:
  CACHIX_SIGNING_KEY: ${{ secrets.CACHIX_SIGNING_KEY }}
  CACHIX_AUTH_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.CACHIX_AUTH_TOKEN }}

# Strategy:
# Job `build_test_drivers` evals all VM tests and builds all test drivers in a single Nix build.
# Compared to launching a separate build job for each VM test, this avoids duplicated
# builds of derivations that the test drivers have in common.
#
# The job matrix `test_scenario` runs the VM tests. Scenario jobs ALWAYS run —
# build-to-cachix.sh early-exits green when a scenario's result is already in
# the binary cache — so every push reports a real conclusion for all five
# checks. (A job-level skip leaves the matrix contexts unreported, and the
# release branch ruleset then blocks fast-forwards forever with
# "required status checks are expected".)
jobs:
  build_test_drivers:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@11d5960a326750d5838078e36cf38b85af677262 # v4
      - uses: cachix/install-nix-action@b85815f71a6de0ddee80b8a80a98d43f4bcc66c7 # v31
      - id: main
        name: Build test drivers
        run: |
          echo "::group::Setup Nix env"
          nix shell --inputs-from . nixpkgs#{bash,coreutils,cachix} -c ./test/ci/build_test_drivers.sh

  test_scenario:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    strategy:
      fail-fast: false
      matrix:
        # `default` (secureNode) and `regtest` boot the full stack
        # (bitcoind + lnd + electrs + btcpayserver/nbxplorer) and assert it
        # works — this catches RPC-whitelist / service-health regressions.
        # `netns` covers network-namespace isolation. `wireguard-lndconnect`
        # boots the wireguard preset and reaches lnd REST through the tunnel
        # from a second VM — the preset's firewall rules are its whole security
        # model, so they are release-gated too (audit 2026-09-09).
        scenario:
          - default
          - regtest
          - netns
          - wireguard-lndconnect
    needs: build_test_drivers
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@11d5960a326750d5838078e36cf38b85af677262 # v4
      # VM tests run QEMU inside the Nix build sandbox and need /dev/kvm
      # accessible to the build users. GitHub runners have KVM hardware; open
      # its permissions so the sandbox can use it.
      - name: Enable KVM
        run: |
          echo 'KERNEL=="kvm", GROUP="kvm", MODE="0666", OPTIONS+="static_node=kvm"' | sudo tee /etc/udev/rules.d/99-kvm4all.rules
          sudo udevadm control --reload-rules
          sudo udevadm trigger --name-match=kvm
      - uses: cachix/install-nix-action@b85815f71a6de0ddee80b8a80a98d43f4bcc66c7 # v31
      - run: nix shell --inputs-from . nixpkgs#{bash,coreutils,cachix} -c ./test/ci/build.sh ${{ matrix.scenario }}

  check_flake:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@11d5960a326750d5838078e36cf38b85af677262 # v4
      - uses: cachix/install-nix-action@b85815f71a6de0ddee80b8a80a98d43f4bcc66c7 # v31
      - run: nix flake check --all-systems
      # FIXME: Re-enable when the nixos-search input is usable again.
      # This is also disabled in ./test/run-tests.sh.
      # - run: ./test/nixos-search/ci-test.sh
      - run: ./test/shellcheck.sh
name: Release tag

# Every push to `release` is a fleet-validated snapshot (the branch ruleset only
# accepts commits with all CI checks green). Tag those snapshots so advisories,
# users, and downstream pins can name a version instead of a bare commit hash.
#
# CalVer (YYYY.MM.DD[.N]): for a security-sensitive fork, "how fresh is this
# pin" is the question a version should answer, and it never collides with
# upstream's frozen v0.0.x line. Only closure-affecting pushes are tagged;
# CI/docs-only fast-forwards are skipped.
on:
  push:
    branches: [release]

permissions:
  contents: write

concurrency:
  group: release-tag
  cancel-in-progress: false

jobs:
  tag:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@11d5960a326750d5838078e36cf38b85af677262 # v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0
      - name: Tag and release if the closure changed
        env:
          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
        run: |
          set -euo pipefail
          git config user.name  "github-actions[bot]"
          git config user.email "41898282+github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com"
          prev=$(git describe --tags --abbrev=0 --match '20*' 2>/dev/null || echo "")

          # Skip when nothing closure-affecting changed since the last release.
          if [ -n "$prev" ]; then
            if git diff --quiet "$prev"..HEAD -- modules pkgs helper overlay.nix default.nix flake.nix flake.lock; then
              echo "No closure-affecting change since $prev — not tagging."
              exit 0
            fi
          fi

          date=$(date -u +%Y.%m.%d)
          tag=$date; n=1
          while git rev-parse -q --verify "refs/tags/$tag" >/dev/null; do
            n=$((n+1)); tag="$date.$n"
          done

          {
            echo "Fleet-validated snapshot of the maintained nix-bitcoin fork."
            echo
            echo "## nixpkgs pins"
            for inp in nixpkgs nixpkgs-unstable; do
              rev=$(jq -r ".nodes.\"$inp\".locked.rev // empty" flake.lock)
              [ -n "$rev" ] && echo "- \`$inp\`: $rev"
            done
            echo
            echo "## Changes since ${prev:-the beginning}"
            if [ -n "$prev" ]; then git log --no-merges --pretty='- %s' "$prev"..HEAD; else echo "- initial tagged release"; fi
          } > notes.md

          git tag -a "$tag" -m "Release $tag"
          git push origin "refs/tags/$tag"
          gh release create "$tag" --title "$tag" --notes-file notes.md
          echo "Released $tag"
name: CVE scan

# Public, auditable security posture for everyone who pins this fork. Weekly
# (and on demand) vulnix scan of the shipped service closure against the NVD.
# New non-whitelisted findings open/update a tracking issue; a clean scan
# closes it. This is the fork-repo analogue of the on-node vulnix scan our own
# fleet runs — see SECURITY.md.
on:
  schedule:
    - cron: "0 6 * * 1" # Mondays 06:00 UTC
  workflow_dispatch:

permissions:
  contents: read
  issues: write

concurrency:
  group: cve-scan
  cancel-in-progress: true

jobs:
  scan:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@11d5960a326750d5838078e36cf38b85af677262 # v4
      - uses: cachix/install-nix-action@b85815f71a6de0ddee80b8a80a98d43f4bcc66c7 # v31
      - name: Use binary cache
        run: |
          mkdir -p ~/.config/nix
          nix run --inputs-from . nixpkgs#cachix -- use ostermayer || true
      - name: Scan
        id: scan
        run: ./test/ci/cve-scan.sh
      - name: Open / update / close tracking issue
        env:
          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
        run: |
          set -euo pipefail
          title="[cve-scan] known CVEs in the shipped closure"
          existing=$(gh issue list --state open --search "$title in:title" \
            --json number --jq '.[0].number' || true)
          if [ "${{ steps.scan.outputs.found }}" = "true" ]; then
            body=$(cat cve-summary.md)
            if [ -n "$existing" ]; then
              gh issue edit "$existing" --body "$body"
              gh issue comment "$existing" --body "Rescanned $(date -u +%F): findings updated above."
            else
              gh issue create --title "$title" --label security --body "$body"
            fi
          elif [ -n "$existing" ]; then
            gh issue comment "$existing" --body "Scan on $(date -u +%F) is clean — closing."
            gh issue close "$existing"
          else
            echo "clean scan, no open issue — nothing to do"
          fi
      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@ea165f8d65b6e75b540449e92b4886f43607fa02 # v4
        if: always()
        with:
          name: vulnix-report
          path: |
            vulnix-report.json
            cve-summary.md
version: 2
updates:
  # Keep GitHub Actions current and SHA-pinned. Our CI is part of the trust
  # chain for everyone who pins this fork, so a compromised action is a
  # supply-chain risk. Dependabot bumps the pinned SHA + version comment when
  # an action releases, and surfaces security advisories against actions.
  - package-ecosystem: github-actions
    directory: /
    schedule:
      interval: weekly
    commit-message:
      prefix: "ci"
    labels:
      - dependencies
# vulnix whitelist for the repo CVE scan (test/ci/cve-scan.sh).
#
# Each entry (a [pname] section) silences findings that are false positives, not
# applicable to how we ship the package, or accepted with a documented reason.
# An entry with no `cve` key whitelists all of that package's current and future
# CVEs — so keep entries NARROW and justified. Format:
# https://github.com/flyingcircusio/vulnix
#
# The scan target (test/ci/scan-node.nix) is a representative node's runtime
# closure. That closure still drags in the BUILD toolchain of some services
# (compilers, build systems, VCS used to fetch sources). Those packages are
# never invoked by a running node service and are not network-reachable, so
# their CVEs are not node attack surface. They are whitelisted below. Everything
# else — glibc, zlib, sqlite, dbus, nghttp2, snappy, openssl, and the like — is
# left REPORTED for real human triage (is our version actually affected? is
# there an upstream fix to pull into pkgs/overrides.nix?).
#
# Reviewed 2026-08-20. Revisit if a service starts invoking one of these at
# runtime (then it becomes attack surface again).
#
# The entries above (build tooling) whitelist a whole pname. The section at the
# BOTTOM is a different category: vulnix pname COLLISIONS — CVEs that belong to
# an unrelated product sharing the package name, and do not apply to the software
# we ship at all. Those are scoped with an explicit `cve = [...]` list (never a
# blanket pname whitelist), so a genuinely-new CVE for the real package still
# reports. Each was verified against NVD on the date noted.
#
# Reviewed 2026-08-24 (collision triage of cve-scan issue #1).

[gcc]
comment = "compiler in the build closure; not run on a deployed node"

[go]
comment = "Go toolchain/bootstrap from a service build; not run on a deployed node"

[cargo]
comment = "Rust build tool (electrs); not run on a deployed node"

[binutils]
comment = "build toolchain; not run on a deployed node"

[ninja]
comment = "build system; not run on a deployed node"

[patch]
comment = "build-time source patching; not run on a deployed node"

[ada]
comment = "build toolchain; not run on a deployed node"

[lapack]
comment = "build-time numeric lib; not part of any node service's runtime path"

[fontforge]
comment = "build-time font tooling (web assets); not run on a deployed node"

[mercurial]
comment = "VCS used to fetch sources at build time; not run on a deployed node"

[ShellCheck]
comment = "build/lint tooling; not run on a deployed node"

[async]
comment = "Haskell build dep (ShellCheck); not run on a deployed node"

[Diff]
comment = "build-time diff lib; not run on a deployed node"

[stringbuilder]
comment = "build-time .NET dep (btcpayserver); not run on a deployed node"

# --- vulnix pname collisions (CVE belongs to an unrelated product) -----------
# These are NOT our software's CVEs. Scoped per-CVE so real future CVEs report.

[git]
cve = ["CVE-2021-21684", "CVE-2022-30947", "CVE-2022-36882", "CVE-2022-36883", "CVE-2022-36884", "CVE-2022-38663"]
comment = "Jenkins Git PLUGIN (org.jenkins-ci.plugins:git) CVEs, not git the VCS in our closure. vulnix name collision — verified via NVD 2026-08-24."

[dash]
cve = ["CVE-2024-21485"]
comment = "Plotly Dash (Python/JS web framework) XSS, not the dash shell (0.5.x). OpenEmbedded/Alpine mark it N/A for the shell. Verified 2026-08-24."

[snappy]
cve = ["CVE-2023-28115", "CVE-2023-41330"]
comment = "KnpLabs knp-snappy (PHP PDF lib) PHAR-deserialization RCE, not google snappy (C++ compression). Name collision — verified 2026-08-24."

[nix]
cve = ["CVE-2024-27297"]
comment = "Nix package MANAGER (2.x) FOD sandbox bypass. The flagged 'nix 0.29.0/0.31.1' is the Rust `nix` crate — version-mismatched name collision. Verified 2026-08-24."

[uuid]
cve = ["CVE-2026-41907", "CVE-2026-41988"]
comment = "JavaScript npm `uuid` library (RFC9562, fixed 14.0.0), not the C/Rust uuid (1.20.0) in this closure. Name collision — verified 2026-08-24."
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# cve-scan.sh — scan the fork's shipped service closure for known CVEs.
     3	#
     4	# We ship NixOS MODULES, not a flat package set, so the honest scan target is a
     5	# built node's RUNTIME closure. test/ci/scan-node.nix builds a representative
     6	# node with every shipped service enabled (bitcoind, lnd, electrs, btcpayserver,
     7	# nbxplorer) and we vulnix its system toplevel — the software a user actually
     8	# runs. (Scanning a test-derivation closure instead would include build-time
     9	# deps like compilers, whose CVEs never reach a deployed node.)
    10	#
    11	# vulnix matches every store path in that closure against the NVD. Findings not
    12	# covered by .github/cve-whitelist.toml are reported; the caller (workflow)
    13	# turns them into a tracking issue.
    14	#
    15	# Usage: ./test/ci/cve-scan.sh [scenario ...]   (default: default regtest)
    16	# Outputs (cwd): vulnix-report.json, cve-summary.md
    17	# Exit: 0 on a completed scan (findings are data, not a failure — the workflow
    18	# decides); nonzero on a FAILED scan (build/vulnix/NVD error), so a broken scan
    19	# fails the job loudly instead of masquerading as "clean" and closing the issue.
    20	set -euo pipefail
    21	
    22	cd "${BASH_SOURCE[0]%/*}/../.."
    23	REPO=$PWD
    24	WHITELIST="$REPO/.github/cve-whitelist.toml"
    25	NIX=(nix --extra-experimental-features "nix-command flakes")
    26	
    27	# Build a representative node's system toplevel and scan its RUNTIME closure —
    28	# the software a user actually runs. Scanning a test-derivation closure instead
    29	# would drag in build-time deps (go-bootstrap, cargo, shellcheck, …) whose CVEs
    30	# never reach a deployed node and would drown the real findings.
    31	echo "::group::build node toplevel"
    32	toplevel=$("${NIX[@]}" build --no-link --print-out-paths --impure -f test/ci/scan-node.nix) \
    33	  || { echo "node toplevel build failed"; echo '[]' > vulnix-report.json; : > cve-summary.md; exit 1; }
    34	echo "$toplevel"
    35	echo "::endgroup::"
    36	
    37	echo "::group::vulnix"
    38	# vulnix exits non-zero when it finds non-whitelisted vulns; that's expected,
    39	# not an error — capture the JSON regardless.
    40	set +e
    41	"${NIX[@]}" shell --inputs-from . nixpkgs#vulnix -c \
    42	  vulnix --json --whitelist "$WHITELIST" "$toplevel" > vulnix-report.json 2> vulnix.err
    43	rc=$?
    44	set -e
    45	cat vulnix.err || true
    46	echo "vulnix exit=$rc"
    47	echo "::endgroup::"
    48	
    49	# vulnix: 0 = clean, 2 = findings, 1 = scan error (NVD fetch, whitelist parse).
    50	# rc=2 is ALSO click's usage-error exit code, so additionally require the output
    51	# to be a JSON array — otherwise an error would read as a clean scan and the
    52	# workflow would close the tracking issue on garbage.
    53	if [ "$rc" -ne 0 ] && [ "$rc" -ne 2 ]; then
    54	  echo "vulnix scan FAILED (rc=$rc) — not reporting a result"; exit 1
    55	fi
    56	jq -e 'type == "array"' vulnix-report.json >/dev/null 2>&1 \
    57	  || { echo "vulnix produced no valid JSON (rc=$rc) — not reporting a result"; exit 1; }
    58	# Shape check on the entries too: a malformed element (e.g. affected_by not an
    59	# array) would make the counting expression below throw, and an error there
    60	# must never read as "0 findings" (second opinion 2026-09-09, low).
    61	jq -e 'all(.[]; type == "object" and ((.affected_by // []) | type == "array") and ((.whitelisted // []) | type == "array"))' vulnix-report.json >/dev/null 2>&1 \
    62	  || { echo "vulnix report entries have an unexpected shape — not reporting a result"; exit 1; }
    63	
    64	# Summarize with the runner's native jq (present on GitHub runners and pop-os).
    65	# vulnix --json fields: `affected_by` holds only the ACTIVE (non-whitelisted)
    66	# CVEs — whitelist-masked ones are moved to the `whitelisted` array, and
    67	# derivations whose CVEs are ALL masked are omitted from the JSON entirely.
    68	# So filter on affected_by alone: also requiring `whitelisted` to be empty
    69	# silently dropped any derivation carrying BOTH a scoped-whitelisted collision
    70	# CVE and a genuinely new one — defeating the per-CVE scoping policy.
    71	real='[.[] | select(((.affected_by // []) | length) > 0)]'
    72	maxcvss='(([.cvssv3_basescore // {} | .[]] | max) // 0)'
    73	count=$(jq "$real | length" vulnix-report.json) \
    74	  || { echo "cannot count findings in vulnix-report.json — not reporting a result"; exit 1; }
    75	[[ $count =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || { echo "non-numeric finding count '$count' — not reporting a result"; exit 1; }
    76	{
    77	  echo "# CVE scan — $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%MZ)"
    78	  echo
    79	  echo "Scanned: representative node toplevel (secure-node preset: tor, operator; bitcoind + lnd + electrs + btcpayserver + nbxplorer + backups), runtime closure"
    80	  echo "Affected store paths with non-whitelisted CVEs: **$count**"
    81	  echo
    82	  if [ "$count" != 0 ]; then
    83	    # shellcheck disable=SC2016  # jq program, not shell — \(...) is jq syntax
    84	    jq -r "$real | map(. + {_m: $maxcvss}) | sort_by(-._m) | .[] | \"## \(.pname // .name) \(.version // \"\") — CVSS \(._m)\n- \((.affected_by // []) | join(\", \"))\"" \
    85	      vulnix-report.json
    86	    echo
    87	    echo "Triage: fix in \`pkgs/overrides.nix\` (version-guarded); whitelist only vulnix"
    88	    echo "pname collisions or CVEs that are N/A to this runtime closure in \`.github/cve-whitelist.toml\`"
    89	    echo "(this scans the node's RUNTIME closure, so build tooling should not appear — if it does, find out why)."
    90	  else
    91	    echo "No action needed."
    92	  fi
    93	} > cve-summary.md
    94	
    95	# Emit ONLY the machine-readable result to $GITHUB_OUTPUT (logs go to stdout).
    96	if [ "$count" != 0 ]; then found=true; else found=false; fi
    97	echo "found=$found" >> "${GITHUB_OUTPUT:-/dev/stdout}"
    98	echo "scan complete: found=$found"
# Representative nix-bitcoin node: the secure-node preset plus every shipped
# service and backups — i.e. the recommended deployment.
#
# CVE-scan target (test/ci/cve-scan.sh): vulnix scans this system's RUNTIME
# closure — the software a user actually runs (bitcoind, lnd, electrs,
# btcpayserver, nbxplorer, kernel, systemd, openssl, …) — NOT build-time
# dependencies like compilers, which are what a test-derivation closure drags
# in and are irrelevant to a deployed node.
#
# Built with:  nix build --impure -f test/ci/scan-node.nix
let
  f = builtins.getFlake (builtins.toString ./../..);
  lib = f.inputs.nixpkgs.lib;
in
(lib.nixosSystem {
  system = "x86_64-linux";
  modules = [
    f.nixosModules.default
    # The recommended deployment, not the bare service set: secure-node pulls
    # in tor (client + onion services — the largest continuously exposed
    # parsing surface on a real node), the operator/doas setup and the preset
    # infrastructure; backups adds duplicity. Without these the scan silently
    # skipped exactly the components a deployed node exposes (audit
    # 2026-09-09, medium).
    "${f.outPath}/modules/presets/secure-node.nix"
    {
      nix-bitcoin.generateSecrets = true;
      services.backups.enable = true;
      services.bitcoind.enable = true;
      services.lnd.enable = true;
      services.electrs.enable = true;
      services.btcpayserver.enable = true;
      services.btcpayserver.lightningBackend = "lnd";
      # Minimal boot/fs so the system evaluates and builds a toplevel.
      boot.loader.grub.enable = false;
      fileSystems."/" = { device = "/dev/disk/by-label/nixos"; fsType = "ext4"; };
      system.stateVersion = "26.05";
    }
  ];
}).config.system.build.toplevel
#!/usr/bin/env bash

# This script can also be run locally for testing:
#   ./build.sh <scenario>
#
# When variable GITHUB_ACTIONS is unset, this script leaves no persistent traces on the host system.

set -euo pipefail

scenario=$1

if [[ -v GITHUB_ACTIONS ]]; then
    if [[ ! -e /dev/kvm ]]; then
        >&2 echo "No KVM available on VM host."
        exit 1
    fi
fi

cd "${BASH_SOURCE[0]%/*}"
exec ./build-to-cachix.sh --expr "(builtins.getFlake (toString ../..)).legacyPackages.\${builtins.currentSystem}.tests.$scenario"
#!/usr/bin/env bash

# Build a single-output derivation and store it in 'cachixCache'.
# Skip the build if it is already cached.
# Accepts the same arguments as nix-instantiate.

set -euo pipefail

# GitHub defines env vars for missing secrets as EMPTY strings; cachix errors
# on an empty CACHIX_SIGNING_KEY ("A signing key must not be empty") instead of
# falling back to CACHIX_AUTH_TOKEN. Drop empty credentials entirely.
[[ ${CACHIX_SIGNING_KEY:-} ]] || unset CACHIX_SIGNING_KEY
[[ ${CACHIX_AUTH_TOKEN:-} ]] || unset CACHIX_AUTH_TOKEN
cachixCache=ostermayer

trap 'echo "Error at ${BASH_SOURCE[0]}:$LINENO"' ERR

tmpDir=$(mktemp -d -p /tmp)
trap 'rm -rf $tmpDir' EXIT

## Instantiate

time nix-instantiate "$@" --add-root "$tmpDir/drv" --indirect > /dev/null
drv=$(realpath "$tmpDir/drv")
echo "instantiated $drv"

outPath=$(nix-store --query "$drv")
if nix path-info --store "https://${cachixCache}.cachix.org" "$outPath" &>/dev/null; then
    echo "$outPath has already been built successfully."
    exit 0
fi

## Build

if [[ -v GITHUB_ACTIONS ]]; then
    # Avoid cachix warning message
    mkdir -p ~/.config/nix && touch ~/.config/nix/nix.conf
    cachix use "$cachixCache"
fi

if [[ ${CACHIX_SIGNING_KEY:-}${CACHIX_AUTH_TOKEN:-} ]]; then
    # Speed up task by uploading store paths as soon as they are created
    buildCmd="cachix watch-exec $cachixCache nix -- build"
else
    buildCmd="nix build"
fi

$buildCmd --out-link "$tmpDir/result" --print-build-logs "$drv^*"

if [[ ${CACHIX_SIGNING_KEY:-}${CACHIX_AUTH_TOKEN:-} ]]; then
    cachix push "$cachixCache" "$outPath"
fi

echo "$outPath"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

cd "${BASH_SOURCE[0]%/*}"

cachixCache=ostermayer

# GitHub defines env vars for missing secrets as EMPTY strings; cachix errors
# on an empty CACHIX_SIGNING_KEY ("A signing key must not be empty") instead of
# falling back to CACHIX_AUTH_TOKEN. Drop empty credentials entirely.
[[ ${CACHIX_SIGNING_KEY:-} ]] || unset CACHIX_SIGNING_KEY
[[ ${CACHIX_AUTH_TOKEN:-} ]] || unset CACHIX_AUTH_TOKEN

# Declare variables for shellcheck
driverDrvs=()
drivers=()
scenarioTests=()

echo '::group::Eval test drivers'
# Call ./test-info.nix
testInfo=$(time nix eval --raw --show-trace ../..#ciTestInfo)
# This sets variables `driverDrvs`, `drivers`, `scenarioTests`
eval "$testInfo"

for i in "${!drivers[@]}"; do
    echo "${drivers[$i]}"
    echo "${driverDrvs[$i]%^*}"
done
echo '::endgroup::'

if nix path-info --store "https://${cachixCache}.cachix.org" "${scenarioTests[@]}" &>/dev/null; then
    echo
    echo "All tests have already been built successfully:"
    printf '%s\n' "${scenarioTests[@]}"
    exit 0
fi

echo "run_scenario_tests=true" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"

## Build test drivers

if nix path-info --store "https://${cachixCache}.cachix.org" "${drivers[@]}" &>/dev/null; then
    echo
    echo "All test drivers have already been built successfully:"
    exit 0
fi

echo '::group::Build test drivers'

if [[ -v GITHUB_ACTIONS ]]; then
    # Avoid cachix warning message
    mkdir -p ~/.config/nix && touch ~/.config/nix/nix.conf
    cachix use "$cachixCache"
fi

if [[ ${CACHIX_SIGNING_KEY:-}${CACHIX_AUTH_TOKEN:-} ]]; then
    # Speed up task by uploading store paths as soon as they are created
    buildCmd="cachix watch-exec $cachixCache nix -- build"
else
    buildCmd="nix build"
fi

$buildCmd --no-link --print-build-logs "${driverDrvs[@]}"

if [[ ${CACHIX_SIGNING_KEY:-}${CACHIX_AUTH_TOKEN:-} ]]; then
    cachix push "$cachixCache" "${drivers[@]}"
fi
pkgs: instantiateTests:

let
  scenarioNames = let
    inherit (pkgs) lib;
    workflowSrc = builtins.readFile ../../.github/workflows/test.yml;
    matches = builtins.match ".*scenario:(([ \n]+-[ ]+[^ \n]+)+).*" workflowSrc;
    scenariosStr = builtins.head matches;
    particles = builtins.split "[ \n]+-[ ]+" scenariosStr;
    # The first split particle is always an empty str
    particles' = builtins.tail particles;
  in
    builtins.filter lib.isString particles';

  # `instantiateTests` prints the test name before evaluating, which is useful for debugging
  ciTests = instantiateTests scenarioNames;
  drivers = map (x: x.driver) ciTests;
  driverDrvs = map (x: ''"${x.drvPath}^*"'') drivers;
in ''
driverDrvs=(
${builtins.concatStringsSep "\n" driverDrvs}

 repo=fort-nix/nix-bitcoin
+
+# This legacy helper fetches an UPSTREAM (fort-nix) signed release tarball. This
+# repository is a maintained FORK; upstream's release artifacts do NOT contain
+# this fork's fixes, so fetching them here would silently ship un-fixed code.
+# Consume this fork via its flake pin instead:
+#   inputs.nix-bitcoin.url = "github:ostermayer/nix-bitcoin/release";
+# Refuse to run unless explicitly overridden.
+if [[ ${NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM:-} != 1 ]]; then
+    echo "This is a fork of nix-bitcoin; pin the flake, don't fetch releases:" >&2
+    echo "  inputs.nix-bitcoin.url = \"github:ostermayer/nix-bitcoin/release\";" >&2
+    echo "This helper fetches UPSTREAM ($repo) releases, WITHOUT the fork's fixes." >&2
+    echo "Set NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM=1 only if you truly want upstream." >&2
+    exit 1
+fi
+
 if [[ ! -v version ]]; then
     version=$(curl -fsS "https://api.github.com/repos/$repo/releases/latest" | jq -r '.tag_name' | tail -c +2)
 fi

     }
 
     update-nix-bitcoin() {(
+      # Fork-native pin: resolve the fork's latest CalVer release tag to the
+      # commit it points at, prefetch that commit's tarball and rewrite
+      # nix-bitcoin-release.nix. Never fetches upstream fort-nix releases
+      # (they lack this fork's fixes). Tags are unsigned CI pointers — the
+      # commit is what you review; see SECURITY.md.
       set -euo pipefail
       releaseFile="${cfgDir}/nix-bitcoin-release.nix"
+      repo=https://github.com/ostermayer/nix-bitcoin
+      tag=$(git ls-remote --tags --refs "$repo" 'refs/tags/20*' | sed 's|.*refs/tags/||' | sort -V | tail -n1)
+      [[ $tag ]] || { echo "No release tag found on $repo" >&2; exit 1; }
+      commit=$(git ls-remote "$repo" "refs/tags/$tag^{}" | cut -f1)
+      [[ $commit ]] || commit=$(git ls-remote "$repo" "refs/tags/$tag" | cut -f1)
+      url="$repo/archive/$commit.tar.gz"
+      hash=$(nix-prefetch-url --unpack "$url" 2>/dev/null)
+      new=$(printf 'builtins.fetchTarball {\n  # fork release %s\n  url = "%s";\n  sha256 = "%s";\n}\n' "$tag" "$url" "$hash")
       current=$(cat "$releaseFile" 2>/dev/null || true)
-      new=$(fetch-release)
-      if [[ $new == $current ]]; then
-        echo "nix-bitcoin-release.nix already contains the latest release"
+      if [[ $new == "$current" ]]; then
+        echo "nix-bitcoin-release.nix already pins $tag ($commit)"
       else
-        echo "$new" > "$releaseFile"
-        echo "Updated nix-bitcoin-release.nix"
-        if [[ $isInteractive ]]; then
-          exec nix-shell
-        fi
+        printf '%s' "$new" > "$releaseFile"
+        echo "Pinned $tag ($commit) in nix-bitcoin-release.nix."
+        echo "Review that commit first — the next nix-shell evaluates the new tree's shellHook:"
+        echo "  https://github.com/ostermayer/nix-bitcoin/compare/$(echo "$current" | sed -n 's|.*/archive/\([0-9a-f]*\)\.tar\.gz.*|\1|p')...$commit"
+        echo "then re-enter the deployment shell with: exit; nix-shell"
+        # Deliberately no automatic `exec nix-shell`: that would run the newly
+        # pinned revision's shell code on this machine before the review the
+        # line above asks for (second opinion 2026-09-09).
       fi
     )}
